, and that the density is differentiable (see proof of
Proposition

4.1
). This concludes the first part of the proof.

For the second statement, we note that for any
Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}
there exists some noise innovation distribution
P_{\tilde{N}}\in\ \mathcal{P}
such that
Q
is the distribution of
\tilde{X}
generated by structural assignments

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{i}:=\tilde{f}_{i}(X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}})+\tilde{N}_{i}=\mathbb{E}[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}]+\tilde{N}_{i},

for all
1\leq j\leq p
and mutually independent noise innovations
\tilde{N}=(\tilde{N}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{N}_{p})\sim P_{\tilde{N}}\in\mathcal{P}^{p}
. Let
q
denote the density of
Q
with respect to the Lebesgue measure and let
q_{\tilde{N}_{i}}
denote the density of
\tilde{N}_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq p
. As
Q
is Markov with respect to
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
and generated by an additive noise model the density factorizes as

\displaystyle q(x)=\prod_{i=1}^{p}q(x_{i}|x_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}})=\prod_{i=1}^{p}q_{\tilde{N}_{i}}(x_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}=x_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}]).

The cross entropy between
P_{X}
and
Q
is given by

\displaystyle h(P_{X},Q)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[-\log\left(q(X)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[-\log\left(q(X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[-\log\left(q_{\tilde{N}_{i}}\left(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}\right]\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}h\left(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}\right],\tilde{N}_{i}\right).

Note that
h(P,Q)=h(P)+D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P\|Q)\geq h(P)
with equality if and only if
Q=P
. Thus, the infimum is attained at noise innovations that are equal in distribution to
X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}]
(which has a density by assumption). That is,

\displaystyle\inf_{Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}}h(P_{X},Q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\inf_{\tilde{N}_{j}\sim P_{\tilde{N}_{j}}\in\mathcal{P}}h\left(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}\right],\tilde{N}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}h\left(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}).

\square

C.50.2
Proofs of Section
4.20

Proof of
Theorem

4.1
:
Assume that
\theta=(\mathcal{G},(f_{i}),P_{N})\in\Theta_{R}
with
\mathcal{G}=(V,\mathcal{E})
. For simplicity of the proof, we assume that
\mathbb{E}[X]=0
such that the edge weight estimators simplify to

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{ji}:=\hat{w}_{\mathrm{G}}(j\to i)=\hat{w}_{ji}(\mathbf{X}_{n},\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n})=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,i}-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}}{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}X_{k,i}^{2}}\right),

for all
j\not=i
. Furthermore, define the Gaussian population (for
i\not=j
) and auxiliary (for
(j\to i)\not\in\mathcal{E}
) edge weights by

\displaystyle w_{ji}:=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]}\right),\quad w^{*}_{ji}:=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-\tilde{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]}\right),

respectively, where
\tilde{\varphi}_{ji}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is a fixed function satisfying

\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{j})-\tilde{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}0.

Furthermore, for any
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}=(V,\tilde{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathcal{T}_{p}
denote

\displaystyle\hat{w}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}):=\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}}\hat{w}_{ji},\quad w(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}):=\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}}w_{ji},\quad w^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}):=\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\setminus\mathcal{E}}w^{*}_{ji}+\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\cap\mathcal{E}}w_{ji},

as the total estimated, population and auxiliary edge weights for
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
. As the conditional expectation minimizes the MSPE among measurable functions, i.e.,
\varphi_{ji}=\argmin_{f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-f(X_{j}))^{2}]
, we especially have, for any
i\not=j
, that

\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-\tilde{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}]\geq\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}].

This construction entails, for any
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}
, that

\displaystyle w^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\geq w(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}),\quad\text{and}\quad w^{*}(\mathcal{G})=w(\mathcal{G}).

(C.6)

4.1
implies that there exists an
m>0
such that

\displaystyle\min_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}\setminus\{\mathcal{G}\}}\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G})=m>0.

(C.7)

Thus, for any
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}\setminus\{\mathcal{G}\}
it holds that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G})+\frac{m}{2}\leq\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\frac{m}{2},

(C.8)

by the identifiability assumption of
Equation

C.7
. Now note that
\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})=w(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})+C
with
C=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\log(\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}])/2
for all
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}
. Hence, we have, for all
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}\setminus\{\mathcal{G}\}
, that

\displaystyle w^{*}(\mathcal{G})-\frac{m}{2}=w(\mathcal{G})+\frac{m}{2}\leq w(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\frac{m}{2}\leq w^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\frac{m}{2},

by the equality and inequalities in (
C.8
) and (
C.6
). Thus, we have that

\displaystyle P(\hat{\mathcal{G}}=\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle=P\left(\argmin_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}=(V,\tilde{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathcal{T}_{p}}\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}}\hat{w}_{\mathrm{G}}(j\to i)=\mathcal{G}\right)

\displaystyle\geq P\left(\bigcap_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}}\left(|\hat{w}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-w^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})|<\frac{m}{2}\right)\right).

We conclude that it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}}|\hat{w}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-w^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}0.

To this end, let
\mathcal{E}^{*}:=\{(j\to i):i,j\in V,i\not=j\}\setminus\mathcal{E}
and note that

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}}|\hat{w}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-w^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}}\bigg(\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\setminus\mathcal{E}}\left|\hat{w}_{ji}-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-\tilde{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\cap\mathcal{E}}\left|\hat{w}_{ji}-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]}\right)\right|\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{(j\to i)\in\mathcal{E}^{*}}\left|\hat{w}_{ji}-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-\tilde{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{(j\to i)\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\hat{w}_{ji}-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]}\right)\right|.

(C.9)

Now consider a fixed term
(j\to i)\in\mathcal{E}
in the second sum of (
C.9
). We can upper bound the absolute difference by

\displaystyle\,\left|\hat{w}_{ji}-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left|\log\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,i}-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}\right)-\log\left(\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}]\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left|\log(\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}])-\log\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}X_{k,i}^{2}\right)\right|.

(C.10)

In the upper bound of (
C.10
), the last absolute difference vanishes in probability due to the law of large numbers and the continuous mapping theorem. The first absolute difference also vanishes by the following arguments. Note that,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,i}-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)\left(\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right).

Hence, it holds that

\displaystyle\,\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,i}-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,j}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,j}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)\left(\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,j}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}},

(C.11)

by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
By the law of large numbers, we have that the first factor of the second term of (
C.11
) converges in probability to a constant,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,j}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\mathbb{E}[X_{1,i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{1,j}))^{2}].

The first term and latter factor of the second term of
Equation

C.11
vanish in probability by assumption. That is, for any
\varepsilon>0
we have that

\displaystyle P\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}\left(\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}\right|>\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle=P\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}\left(\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}\right|\land\varepsilon>\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\varphi_{ji}(X_{k,j})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}\right)\land\varepsilon\right]}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\varphi_{ji}(X_{1,j})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{1,j})\right)^{2}\big|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\land\varepsilon\right]}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\to_{n}0,

using conditional Jensen’s inequality (
x\mapsto\min(x,\varepsilon)=x\land\varepsilon
is concave) and the dominated convergence theorem.
This proves that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,j}-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\mathbb{E}[X_{1,i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{1,j}))^{2}].

Thus, we have shown that the second term of (
C.9
) converges to zero in probability. Finally, the above arguments apply similarly to the first term of
Equation

C.9
by exchanging every
\varphi_{ji}
with
\tilde{\varphi}_{ji}
. We have shown that
\sup_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}}|\hat{w}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-w^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}0
, which concludes the proof.

\square

Proof of
Theorem

4.2
:

Assume that for each sample size
n\in\mathbb{N}
that
\theta_{n}=(\mathcal{G},...)\in\Theta_{R}
with
\mathcal{G}=(V,\mathcal{E})
and identifiability gap

\displaystyle\min_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}\setminus\{\mathcal{G}\}}\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})=q_{n}>0,

with
q_{n}^{-1}=o(\sqrt{n})
.
For simplicity of the proof, we assume that
\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[X]=0
such that the edge weight estimators simplify to

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{ji}:=\hat{w}_{\mathrm{G}}(j\to i)=\hat{w}_{ji}(\mathbf{X}_{n},\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n})=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,i}-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}}{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}X_{k,i}^{2}}\right).

Furthermore, we continue with the notation and population quantities introduced in the proof of
Theorem

4.1
, i.e.,
w_{ji}=\log(\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}|X_{j}])^{2}])/\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[X_{i}^{2}])/2
, where we notionally have suppressed the dependence on
n
.
We know that for each SCM
\theta_{n}
it holds that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G})+q_{n}\leq\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}),\quad\text{hence}\quad w(\mathcal{G})+q_{n}\leq w(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}),

for all
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}\setminus\{\mathcal{G}\}
. Thus,

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\argmin_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}=(V,\tilde{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathcal{T}_{p}}\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}}\hat{w}_{ji}=\mathcal{G}\right)

\displaystyle\geq P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\left(|\hat{w}(\mathcal{G})-w(\mathcal{G})|<\frac{q_{n}}{2}\right)\cap\bigcap_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}\setminus\{\mathcal{G}\}}\left(\hat{w}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-w(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\geq-\frac{q_{n}}{2}\right)\right).

For any
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}=(V,\tilde{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathcal{T}_{p}
we have that

\displaystyle\hat{w}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-w(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\cap\mathcal{E}}\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}+\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\setminus\mathcal{E}}\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji},

where
\hat{w}_{ji}
and
w_{ji}
denote the estimated and population Gaussian weights for the edge
(j\to i)
, respectively. Hence, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\forall(j\to i)\in\mathcal{E},\forall\varepsilon>0:P_{\theta_{n}}(|\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}|<q_{n}\varepsilon)\to_{n}1,

\displaystyle\forall(j\to i)\not\in\mathcal{E},\forall\varepsilon>0:P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}\geq-q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\to_{n}1.

To see this, note that if the above statements hold, then

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}\left(|\hat{w}(\mathcal{G})-w(\mathcal{G})|<\frac{q_{n}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\sum_{(j\to i)\in\mathcal{E}}|\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}|<\frac{q_{n}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\bigcap_{(j\to i)\in\mathcal{E}}\left(|\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}|<\frac{q_{n}}{2(p-1)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\to_{n}1,

and for any
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}=(V,\tilde{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathcal{T}_{p}

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\hat{w}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-w(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\geq-\frac{q_{n}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\cap\mathcal{E}}\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}+\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\setminus\mathcal{E}}\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}\geq-\frac{q_{n}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\bigcap_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\cap\mathcal{E}}\left(|\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}|\leq\frac{q_{n}}{2(p-1)}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.\cap\bigcap_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\setminus\mathcal{E}}\left(\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}\geq-\frac{q_{n}}{2(p-1)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\to_{n}1,

hence the probability of the intersections also converges to one.

The causal edges:

Now fix
(j\to i)\in\mathcal{E}
. We want to show that for all
\varepsilon>0
it holds that

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}(|\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}|<q_{n}\varepsilon)\to_{n}1.

First note that

\displaystyle\left|\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left|\log\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,i}-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}\right)-\log\left(\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(X_{i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}]\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left|\log(\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[X_{i}^{2}])-\log\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}X_{k,i}^{2}\right)\right|,

where
\hat{\varphi}_{ji}
for each
n
is the estimated conditional expectation
x\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[X_{i}|X_{j}=x]
based on samples from the auxiliary data set.
It suffices to show the wanted convergence in probability for each of the above terms. Furthermore, for all sequences of positive random variables
(Z_{n})
and positive constants
c>0
and for all
\varepsilon>0
there exists
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle(q_{n}^{-1}|\log(Z_{n})-\log(c)|\geq\varepsilon)\subseteq(q_{n}^{-1}|Z_{n}-c|\geq\delta),

for sufficiently large
n
. To see this, note that if
q_{n}^{-1}(\log(Z_{n})-\log(c))\geq\varepsilon
, then
Z_{n}>\exp(\log(c)+q_{n}\varepsilon)=c\exp(q_{n}\varepsilon)\geq c(1+q_{n}\varepsilon)
, so
q_{n}^{-1}(Z_{n}-c)\geq c\varepsilon
. On the other hand, if
q_{n}^{-1}(\log(Z_{n})-\log(c))\leq-\varepsilon
, then
Z_{n}\leq c\exp(-\varepsilon q_{n})\leq c(1-\varepsilon q_{n}+\varepsilon^{2}q_{n}^{2})
, so
q_{n}^{-1}(Z_{n}-c)\leq-c\varepsilon+c\varepsilon^{2}q_{n}
. In summary, if
q_{n}^{-1}|\log(Z_{n})-\log(c)|\geq\varepsilon
, then
q_{n}^{-1}|Z_{n}-c|\geq c\varepsilon-c\varepsilon^{2}q_{n}>c\varepsilon(1-M)=:\delta
where
1>M>\varepsilon q_{n}
for sufficiently large
n
. We conclude that it suffices to show that for all
\varepsilon>0
it holds that

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,i}-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(X_{i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}]\right|\geq q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\to_{n}0

(C.12)

and that

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}X_{k,i}^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[X_{i}^{2}]\right|\geq q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\to_{n}0,

(C.13)

Equation

C.13
is satisfied as the summands are mean zero i.i.d. Therefore, with

\displaystyle W_{n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}X_{k,i}^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[X_{i}^{2}],

where
\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[q_{n}^{-1}W_{n}]=0
, we have that
\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[q_{n}^{-2}W_{n}^{2}]=\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(X_{i}^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[X_{i}^{2}])^{2}]
, hence

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}(q_{n}^{-1}W_{n}\geq\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq q_{n}^{-2}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[W_{n}^{2}]}{\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}\frac{\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(X_{i}^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[X_{i}^{2}])^{2}]}{\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\to_{n}0,

for any
\varepsilon>0
as
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\|X\|_{2}^{4}<\infty
and
q_{n}^{-1}=o(\sqrt{n})
.

Now we show
Equation

C.12
. First, we simplify the notation by letting
Z_{k}:=X_{k,i}
,
Y_{k}:=X_{k,j}

f:=\varphi_{ji}
and
\hat{f}:=\hat{\varphi}_{ji}
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Note that we have suppressed the dependence of
f=\varphi_{ji}
on
\theta_{n}
. We have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(Z_{k}-\hat{f}(Y_{k})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(Z_{k}-f(Y_{k}))^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(Z_{k}-f(Y_{k}))(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))

\displaystyle=:T_{1,n}+T_{2,n}+T_{3,n}.

It suffices to show that for all
\varepsilon>0
it holds that

(a)

P_{\theta_{n}}\left(|T_{1,n}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(Z_{1}-f(Y_{1}))^{2}]|\geq q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\to_{n}0,

(b)

P_{\theta_{n}}\left(|T_{2,n}|\geq q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\to_{n}0,
and

(c)

P_{\theta_{n}}\left(|T_{3,n}|\geq q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\to_{n}0.

First we show (a). Each term in the sum of
T_{1,n}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(Z_{1}-f(Y_{1}))^{2}]
is mean zero and i.i.d., i.e.,

\displaystyle q_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[\left(Z_{k}-f(Y_{k})\right)^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(Z_{1}-f(Y_{1}))^{2}]]=0.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}_{\theta_{n}}(q_{n}^{-1}(T_{1,n}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(Z_{1}-f(Y_{1}))^{2}]))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}_{\theta_{n}}\left(\frac{q_{n}^{-1}}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(Z_{k}-f(Y_{k})\right)^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(Z_{1}-f(Y_{1}))^{2}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathrm{Var}_{\theta_{n}}\left(\left(Z_{k}-f(Y_{k})\right)^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(Z_{1}-f(Y_{1}))^{2}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathrm{Var}_{\theta_{n}}\left(\left(Z_{1}-f(Y_{1})\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\to_{n}

\displaystyle 0,

since
q_{n}^{-1}=o(\sqrt{n})
and
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\|X\|_{2}^{4}<\infty
. Hence,

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}\left(|q_{n}^{-1}(T_{1,n}-\mathbb{E}[(Z_{1}-f(Y_{1}))^{2}])|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{Var}_{\theta_{n}}(q_{n}^{-1}(T_{,n}-\mathbb{E}[(Z_{1}-f(Y_{1}))^{2}]))}{\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\to_{n}0.

by Chebyshev’s inequality, proving (a).

Now we show (b). To that end, note that the terms of
T_{2,n}
is i.i.d. conditional on
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
. For a fixed
1>\varepsilon>0
we have

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}\left(|q_{n}^{-1}T_{2,n}|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[P_{\theta_{n}}\left(q_{n}^{-1}T_{2,n}\geq\varepsilon|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right)\land 1\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[q_{n}^{-1}T_{2,n}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\land 1\right]}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[q_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{1})-\hat{f}(Y_{1}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\land 1\right]}{\varepsilon},

where we used the conditional Markov’s inequality. Now fix
1>\delta>0
and define
A_{n,\delta}:=(q_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{1})-\hat{f}(Y_{1}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]>\delta)
and note that by assumption there exists an
N_{\delta}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\forall n\geq N_{\delta}:P_{\theta_{n}}(A_{n,\delta})<\delta
. Hence, for
n\geq N_{\delta}
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[q_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{1})-\hat{f}(Y_{1}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\land 1\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[1_{A_{n,\delta}}q_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{1})-\hat{f}(Y_{1}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\land 1\right]

(C.14)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[1_{A_{n,\delta}^{c}}q_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{1})-\hat{f}(Y_{1}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\land 1\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[1_{A_{n,\delta}}q_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{1})-\hat{f}(Y_{1}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\land 1\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[1_{A_{n,\delta}^{c}}\delta\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[1_{A_{n,\delta}}\right]+\delta

\displaystyle=P_{\theta_{n}}(A_{n,\delta})+\delta<2\delta,

(C.15)

hence
\limsup_{n\to\infty}P_{\theta_{n}}\left(|q_{n}^{-1}T_{2,n}|\geq\varepsilon\right)<2\delta/\varepsilon
, i.e.,
P_{\theta_{n}}\left(|q_{n}^{-1}T_{2,n}|\geq\varepsilon\right)\to 0
as
\delta>0
was chosen arbitrarily, proving (b).

Now we prove (c). To this end, recall that

\displaystyle T_{3,n}:=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(Z_{k}-f(Y_{k}))(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k})),

is, conditional on
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}
, an i.i.d. sum with conditional mean zero

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[T_{3,n}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(Z_{k}-f(Y_{k}))(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[Z_{k}|Y_{k},\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]-f(Y_{k}))(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(f(Y_{k})-f(Y_{k}))(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]=0,

and conditional second moment given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[T_{3,n}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{n^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(Z_{k}-f(Y_{k}))^{2}(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(Z_{k}-f(Y_{k}))^{2}(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(Z_{k}-f(Y_{k}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n},Y_{k}\right](f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[\mathrm{Var}_{\theta_{n}}(Z_{k}|Y_{k})(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right],

P_{\theta_{n}}
-almost surely. Hence, w.l.o.g. assume that
0<\varepsilon<1
and note that the conditional Markov’s inequality yields

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}(|q_{n}^{-1}T_{3,n}|\geq\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[P_{\theta_{n}}(|q_{n}^{-1}T_{3,n}|\geq\varepsilon|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n})\land 1]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[q_{n}^{-2}T_{3,n}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\land 1\right]

(C.16)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\land 1\right].

By conditional Jensen’s inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1+\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{4}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right].

Fix
\delta>0
. Let
A_{n,\delta}:=\left(\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{4}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]>\delta\right)
and note that
P_{\theta_{n}}(A_{n,\delta})\to_{n}0
, hence there exists an
N_{\delta}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\forall n\geq N_{\delta}:P_{\theta_{n}}(A_{n,\delta})<\delta
. Furthermore, as
q_{n}^{-1}=o(\sqrt{n})
there exists an
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
q_{n}^{-2}/n<\delta
for all
n\geq N
. Similar to the arguments in
Equation

C.15
we then have that

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{C}P_{\theta_{n}}(|q_{n}^{-1}T_{3,n}|\geq\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}\left(1+\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{4}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\right)\land 1\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}+E_{\theta_{n}}\left[\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{4}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\land 1\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}+\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[1_{A_{n,\delta}}]+\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[1_{A_{n,\delta}^{c}}\delta]

\displaystyle<\delta+P_{\theta_{n}}(A_{n,\delta})+\delta<3\delta,

for any
n\geq N_{\delta}\lor N
, so
P_{\theta_{n}}(q_{n}^{-1}T_{3,n}\geq\varepsilon)\to_{n}0
, proving (c).

The non-causal edges:

Now fix
(j\to i)\not\in\mathcal{E}
, we want to show, for any
\varepsilon>0
that

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}(\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}\geq-q_{n}\varepsilon)\to_{n}1,

where

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\left[\log\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,i}-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}\right)-\log\left(\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}]\right)\right]\right.

\displaystyle\quad+\left.\left[\log(\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}])-\log\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}X_{k,i}^{2}\right)\right]\right)=:\frac{1}{2}(D_{1,n}+D_{2,n}).

We have that
P_{\theta_{n}}(\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}\geq-q_{n}\varepsilon)\geq P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\left(D_{1,n}\geq-q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\cap\left(|D_{2,n}|<q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\right)
,
where the second event has already been shown to have probability converging to one in
Equation

C.13
. Thus, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}\left(D_{1,n}\geq-q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\to_{n}1.

By similar arguments as above we have for any sequence of positive random variables
(K_{n})_{n\geq 1}
and a positive constant
K
that for all
\varepsilon>0
there exists an
\delta>0
such that
P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\log(K_{n})-\log(K)<-q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\leq P_{\theta_{n}}(K_{n}-K<-q_{n}\delta)
,
for sufficiently large
n\in\mathbb{N}
. To see this, note that if
\log(K_{n})-\log(K)<-q_{n}\varepsilon
, then
K_{n}<K\exp(-\varepsilon q_{n})\leq K(1-\varepsilon q_{n}+\varepsilon^{2}q_{n}^{2})
, so
q_{n}^{-1}(K_{n}-K)<-K\varepsilon+K\varepsilon^{2}q_{n}<-K\varepsilon(1-M)=:-\delta
where
1>M>\varepsilon q_{n}
for sufficiently large
n
, since
q_{n}\downarrow 0
. Thus, it suffices to show that for any
\varepsilon>0
it holds that

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(X_{k,i}-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{k,j})\right)^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(X_{i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}]\geq-q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\to_{n}1.

Again, we simplify the notation
Z_{k}:=X_{k,i}
,
Y_{k}:=X_{k,j}
,
f=\varphi_{ji}
and
\hat{f}:=\hat{\varphi}_{ji}
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Now define the following terms

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(Z_{k}-\hat{f}(Y_{k})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(Z_{k}-f(Y_{k}))^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\{(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{2}-\delta_{n,\theta_{n}}^{2}\}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\{(Z_{k}-f(Y_{k}))(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))+\delta_{n,\theta_{n}}^{2}/2\}

\displaystyle=:T_{1,n}+\tilde{T}_{2,n}+\tilde{T}_{3,n},

where
\delta_{n,\theta_{n}}^{2}:=\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(f(Y_{1})-\hat{f}(Y_{1}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]=\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(\varphi_{ji}(X_{j})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}(X_{j}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]
. It suffices to show that for all
\varepsilon>0
it holds that

(d)

P_{\theta_{n}}\left(|T_{1,n}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[(Z_{1}-f(Y_{1}))^{2}]|\geq q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\to_{n}0,

(e)

P_{\theta_{n}}\left(|\tilde{T}_{2,n}|\geq q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\to_{n}0,
and

(f)

P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\tilde{T}_{3,n}\geq-q_{n}\varepsilon\right)\to_{n}1.

Condition (d) holds by arguments similar to (a) for the causal edges.

Now we prove (e).
The expansion, conditional on
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
, is a sum of mean zero i.i.d. terms, hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left(\left.q_{n}^{-2}\tilde{T}_{2,n}^{2}\right|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[\{(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{2}-\delta_{n,\theta_{n}}^{2}\}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{4}+(\delta_{n,\theta_{n}}^{2})^{2}-2(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{2}\delta_{n,\theta_{n}}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{4}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]-(\delta_{n,\theta_{n}}^{2})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{4}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right],

using that
(\delta_{n,\theta_{n}}^{2})^{2}\geq 0
. Fix
1>\delta>0
and let

A_{n,\delta}:=\left(\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{4}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]>\delta\right),

and note that there exists an
N_{\delta}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\forall n\geq N_{\delta}:P_{\theta_{n}}(A_{n,\delta})<\delta
. Similar to the previous arguments we have for any
1>\varepsilon>0
and
n\geq N_{\delta}
that

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{2,n}\right|\geq q_{n}\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[P_{\theta_{n}}\left(\left.\left|q_{n}^{-1}\tilde{T}_{2,n}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right)\land 1\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[q_{n}^{-2}\tilde{T}_{2,n}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\land 1\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[\frac{q_{n}^{-2}}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k}))^{4}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\land 1\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[1_{A_{n,\delta}}]+\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}[1_{A_{n,\delta}^{c}}\delta]\right)<\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon^{2}},

by the conditional Markov’s inequality. Since
\delta>0
was chosen arbitrarily, we conclude that (e) holds.

Finally we show (f). Recall that in the analysis of the causal edges, we defined

\displaystyle T_{3,n}:=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(Z_{k}-f(Y_{k}))(f(Y_{k})-\hat{f}(Y_{k})).

Hence, we have that
\tilde{T}_{3,n}=T_{3,n}+\delta_{n,\theta_{n}}^{2}
. We realize that for any
0<\varepsilon<1

\displaystyle P_{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{T}_{3,n}<-q_{n}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq P_{\theta_{n}}(T_{3,n}+\delta_{n,\theta_{n}}^{2}\leq-q_{n}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=P_{\theta_{n}}\left(T_{3,n}\leq-\left(q_{n}\varepsilon+\delta_{n,\theta_{n}}^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq P_{\theta_{n}}\left(T_{3,n}^{2}\geq\left(q_{n}\varepsilon+\delta_{n,\theta_{n}}^{2}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq P_{\theta_{n}}\left(T_{3,n}^{2}\geq\left(q_{n}\varepsilon\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=P_{\theta_{n}}\left(q_{n}^{-2}T_{3,n}^{2}\geq\varepsilon^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[P_{\theta_{n}}\left(q_{n}^{-2}T_{3,n}^{2}\geq\varepsilon^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right)\land 1\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[\left.q_{n}^{-2}T_{3,n}^{2}\right|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right]\land 1\right]

\displaystyle\to_{n}0,

where we used the convergence shown in the proof of (c); see
Equation

C.16
. To see that the former arguments apply to non-causal edges, simply note that they did not use any conditions restricted to causal edges.
This concludes the proof.

\square

C.50.3
Proofs of Section
4.21

Lemma C.1
.

Consider an i.i.d. sequence
(X_{m})_{m\geq 1}
of random variables with
X_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
independent from a random infinite sequence
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\in\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
(\psi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be a sequence of measurable functions s.t. for all
n\geq 1
,
\psi_{n}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}^{q}
satisfies the following conditions:

(a)

\mathbb{E}[\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]=0
almost surely,

(b)

\exists\,\Sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times q}:\sum_{m=1}^{n}\mathrm{Var}(\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})|\tilde{\mathbf{X}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\Sigma
, and

(c)

\exists\,\varepsilon>0:\sum_{m=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\|\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})\|_{2}^{2+\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}0
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{n}\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma),

Proof of
Lemma

C.1
:

Let the random sequences be defined on a common probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P)
and define

\displaystyle A_{nm}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}[\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}],

\displaystyle B_{n}

\displaystyle:=\Sigma-\sum_{m=1}^{n}\mathrm{Var}(\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}),

\displaystyle C_{n}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{m=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\|\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})\|_{2}^{2+\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}].

By assumption we have that
P(\cap_{n,m}(A_{nm}=0))=1,B_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0
and
C_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0
as
n\to\infty
. First, note that for any subsequence
(n_{k})_{k\geq 1}
of the positive integers, there exists a subsequence
(n_{k_{l}})_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

\displaystyle P(\lim_{l\to\infty}B_{n_{k_{l}}}=0)=1\quad\text{for}\quad(\lim_{l\to\infty}B_{n_{k_{l}}}=0)

\displaystyle:=\{\omega\in\Omega:\lim_{l\to\infty}B_{n_{k_{l}}}(\omega)=0\},

and

\displaystyle P(\lim_{l\to\infty}C_{n_{k_{l}}}=0)=1\quad\text{for}\quad(\lim_{l\to\infty}C_{n_{k_{l}}}=0)

\displaystyle:=\{\omega\in\Omega:\lim_{l\to\infty}C_{n_{k_{l}}}(\omega)=0\}.

Thus, define

\displaystyle G:=(\cap_{n,m}(A_{nm}=0)\cap(\lim_{l\to\infty}B_{n_{k_{l}}}=0)\cap(\lim_{l\to\infty}C_{n_{k_{l}}}=0))\subseteq\Omega,\quad\text{with}\quad P(G)=1.

Now fix
\tilde{x}\in\tilde{\mathbf{X}}(G):=\{\tilde{\mathbf{X}}(\omega)\in\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{R}^{d}:\omega\in G\}
and note that

\displaystyle\forall l\geq 1,\forall 1\leq m\leq n_{k_{l}}:\mathbb{E}[\psi_{n_{k_{l}}}(X_{m},\tilde{x})]=0,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{n_{k_{l}}}\mathrm{Var}(\psi_{n_{k_{l}}}(X_{m},\tilde{x}))\to_{l}\Sigma,\text{ and}

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{n_{k_{l}}}\mathbb{E}[\|\psi_{n_{k_{l}}}(X_{m},\tilde{x})\|_{2}^{2+\varepsilon}]\to_{l}0.

Furthermore, for any
l\geq 1

\displaystyle\psi_{n_{k_{l}}}(X_{1},\tilde{x}),...,\psi_{n_{k_{l}}}(X_{n_{k_{l}}},\tilde{x}),\quad\text{are jointly independent},

hence by Lyapunov’s central limit theorem for triangular arrays (see, e.g.,
Van der Vaart 2000
, Proposition 2.27, and recall that Lyapunov’s condition implies the Lindeberg–Feller condition) that

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{n_{k_{l}}}\psi_{n_{k_{l}}}(X_{m},\tilde{x})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{l}Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma).

The above convergence in distribution is equivalent to the following statement: for any continuous bounded function
g:\mathbb{R}^{q}\to\mathbb{R}
it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{l\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[g\left(\sum_{m=1}^{n_{k_{l}}}\psi_{n_{k_{l}}}(X_{m},\tilde{x})\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[g(Z)\right].

Fix a continuous and bounded
g
and note that the above convergence holds for all
\tilde{x}\in\tilde{\mathbf{X}}(G)
with
P(G)=1
. Thus, it must hold that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[g\left(\sum_{m=1}^{n_{k_{l}}}\psi_{n_{k_{l}}}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})\right)\big|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{l}\mathbb{E}\left[g(Z)\right].

Finally, as
(n_{k_{l}})_{l\geq 1}
is a subsequence of an arbitrary subsequence of positive integers, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[g\left(\sum_{m=1}^{n}\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{x})\right)\big|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[g(Z)\right],

and since
g
is bounded the dominated convergence theorem yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[g\left(\sum_{m=1}^{n}\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[g\left(\sum_{m=1}^{n}\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})\right)\big|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right]\right]\to_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[g(Z)\right].

As
g
was chosen arbitrarily, the above convergence holds for any continuous bounded
g
. We conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{n}\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma),

proving the theorem.

\square

Lemma C.2

(
Shah and Peters 2020
, Lemma 19)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a family of distributions for a random variable
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
and suppose
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\ldots
are i.i.d. copies of
\zeta
. For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
let
S_{n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\zeta_{i}.
Suppose that for all
P\in\mathcal{P}
we have
\mathbb{E}_{P}(\zeta)=0
and
\mathbb{E}_{P}\left(|\zeta|^{1+\eta}\right)<c
for some
\eta,c>0.
We have that for all
\varepsilon>0
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}P\left(\left|S_{n}\right|>\varepsilon\right)=0.

Lemma C.3
.

Let
U
be a random element and let
(Z_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be an i.i.d. sequence of random variables such that
U\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}(Z_{n})_{n\geq 1}
and let
\left((W_{nm})_{m\leq n}\right)_{n\geq 1}
be a triangular array of random variables and
(g_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be measurable mappings with the following properties:

(a)

\forall n\geq 1,\forall m\leq n:W_{nm}=g_{n}(Z_{m},U)
,

(b)

\exists\eta>0:\mathbb{E}\left(\left|W_{n1}\right|^{1+\eta}\mid U\right)=O_{p}(1)
, as
n\to\infty
.

Then, writing
\bar{W}_{n}:=\sum_{m=1}^{n}W_{nm}/n
, we have

\left|\bar{W}_{n}-\mathbb{E}\left(W_{n1}\mid U\right)\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}0.

Proof of
Lemma

C.3
:
Denote

\displaystyle j_{n}(Z_{m},U):=g_{n}(Z_{m},U)-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(Z_{1},U)|U],

for any
n\geq 1
and
m\leq n
. Let
\delta>0
be given. Pick
M>0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that the events

\Omega_{n}:=\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|g_{n}(Z_{1},U)\right|^{1+\eta}\mid U\right]\leq M\right\},

satisfy
\mathbb{P}\left(\Omega_{n}^{c}\right)<\delta
for
n\geq N
. Notice that

\displaystyle U(\Omega_{n})=\left\{\tilde{u}_{n}:\mathbb{E}\left[\left|g_{n}(Z_{1},\tilde{u}_{n})\right|^{1+\eta}\right]\leq M\right\},

since
U\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}(Z_{n})_{n\geq 1}
. Fix
\varepsilon>0
. Then, for all
n\geq N

\displaystyle P\left(\left|\bar{W}_{n}-\mathbb{E}\left(W_{n}\mid U\right)\right|>\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle=P\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}j_{n}(Z_{m},U)\right|>\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle<\mathbb{E}\left[P\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}j_{n}(Z_{m},U)\right|>\varepsilon\mid U\right)1_{\Omega_{n}}\right]+\delta.

By the dominated convergence theorem, the first term on the RHS converges to 0 if

\displaystyle\,\sup_{\omega\in\Omega_{n}}P\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}j_{n}(Z_{m},U)\right|>\varepsilon\mid U\right)(\omega)

\displaystyle\,=\sup_{\tilde{u}_{n}\in U(\Omega_{n})}P\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}j_{n}(Z_{m},\tilde{u}_{n})\right|>\varepsilon\right)\rightarrow_{n}0,

which implies the desired statement as
\delta>0
was chosen arbitrarily. Now note that for any
n\in\mathbb{N},\tilde{u}_{n}\in U(\Omega_{n})
and all
m\in\mathbb{N}
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|j_{n}(Z_{m},\tilde{u}_{n})|^{1+\eta}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[|g_{n}(Z_{m},\tilde{u}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(Z_{1},\tilde{u}_{n})]|^{1+\eta}]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\eta}\left(\mathbb{E}[|g_{n}(Z_{m},\tilde{u}_{n})|^{1+\eta}]+|\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(Z_{1},\tilde{u}_{n})]|^{1+\eta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\eta}\left(\mathbb{E}[|g_{n}(Z_{m},\tilde{u}_{n})|^{1+\eta}]+\mathbb{E}[|g_{n}(Z_{1},\tilde{u}_{n})|^{1+\eta}]\right)

\displaystyle<2^{\eta+1}M=:c

by the cr and Jensen’s inequalities, and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[j_{n}(Z_{m},\tilde{u}_{n})]=0.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, define the following set of pushforward measures

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{n}:=\{P^{\prime}=(j_{n}(Z_{1},\tilde{u}_{n}))(P):\tilde{u}_{n}\in U(\Omega_{n})\}.

For any
P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}
, let
(Y_{m})_{m\geq 1}
be a sequence of i.i.d. random variables such that
Y_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}}{{=}}j_{n}(Z_{1},\tilde{u}_{n})
for some
\tilde{u}_{n}\in U(\Omega_{n})
. Notice that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}
it holds that
\mathbb{E}_{P^{\prime}}|Y_{1}|^{1+\eta}<c
and
\mathbb{E}_{P^{\prime}}[Y_{1}]=0
. Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{u}_{n}\in U(\Omega_{n})}P\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}j_{n}(Z_{m},\tilde{u}_{n})\right|>\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\sup_{P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}P^{\prime}\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}Y_{m}\right|>\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{P^{\prime}\in\cup_{k}\mathcal{P}_{k}}P^{\prime}\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}Y_{m}\right|>\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\to_{n}0,

by the weak uniform law of large numbers,
Lemma

C.2
.

\square

Lemma C.4

(Asymptotic normality of edge weight components)
.

Let for each sample size
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\hat{\varphi}^{n}_{ji}
denote the estimated conditional mean function
\varphi_{ji}
based on the auxiliary sample
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
.
For any
j\not=i
and
m\leq n
, define

\displaystyle\hat{R}_{nm,ji}:=\{X_{m,i}-\hat{\varphi}^{n}_{ji}(X_{m,j})\},\quad\quad\quad\quad

\displaystyle\hat{\mu}_{n,ji}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2},

\displaystyle R_{m,ji}:=\{X_{m,i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{m,j})\},

\displaystyle\mu_{ji}:=\mathbb{E}[R_{1,ji}^{2}],

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{m,i}:=\left(X_{m,i}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}X_{k,i}\right)^{2},

\displaystyle\hat{\nu}_{n,i}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\hat{V}_{m,i},

\displaystyle\nu_{i}:=\mathrm{Var}(X_{1,i}),

\displaystyle\delta_{n,ji}^{2}:=\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\varphi}^{n}_{ji}(X_{1,j})-\varphi_{ji}(X_{1,j}))^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}].

Let

\displaystyle\widehat{\Sigma}_{n}

\displaystyle:=\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,R}&\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,RV}\\
\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,RV}^{\intercal}&\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,V}\end{bmatrix}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{R}_{nm}^{2}(\hat{R}_{nm}^{2})^{\intercal}-\hat{\mu}_{n}\hat{\mu}_{n}^{\intercal}&\hat{R}_{nm}^{2}\hat{V}_{m}^{\intercal}-\hat{\mu}_{n}\hat{\nu}_{n}^{\intercal}\\
\hat{V}_{m}(\hat{R}_{nm}^{2})^{\intercal}-\hat{\nu}_{n}\hat{\mu}_{n}^{\intercal}&\hat{V}_{m}\hat{V}_{m}^{\intercal}-\hat{\nu}_{n}\hat{\nu}_{n}^{\intercal}\end{bmatrix},

denote the
p^{2}\times p^{2}
matrix empirical covariance matrix,
where the squaring of vectors means that each entry is squared.
Suppose there exists
\xi>0
such that for all
j\not=i
, the following three conditions hold:

(i)

\mathbb{E}\|X\|^{4+\xi}<\infty
.

(ii)

\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\varphi}^{n}_{ji}(X_{j})-\varphi_{ji}(X_{j})|^{4+\xi}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]=O_{p}(1)
, as
n\to\infty
.

(iii)

\exists\Sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{p^{2}\times p^{2}}:\mathrm{Var}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\hat{R}_{n1}^{2}-\delta_{n}^{2}-\mu\\
\hat{V}_{1}-\nu\end{bmatrix}\bigg|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\Sigma
, where
\Sigma
is constant.

Then we have that
\widehat{\Sigma}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{p^{2}\times p^{2}}
and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{R}_{nm}^{2}-\delta_{n}^{2}-\mu\\
\hat{V}_{m}-\nu\end{bmatrix}=\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\mu}_{n}-\delta_{n}^{2}-\mu\\
\hat{\nu}_{n}-\nu\end{bmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma).

(C.17)

Proof of
Lemma

C.4
:

We prove the lemma under the assumption that
\mathbb{E}[X]=0
under which the variance estimator simplify to
\hat{V}_{m,i}:=X_{m,i}^{2}
and
\hat{\nu}_{n,i}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\hat{V}_{m,i}
for all
1\leq i\leq p
. The proof only gets more notionally cumbersome without this assumption. It should follow in all generality by applying expansion techniques and Slutsky’s theorem similar to the standard arguments showing asymptotic normality of the regular sample variance.

Let
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}
denote the auxilliary i.i.d. process such that
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
is the first
n
-coordinates of said process.
Note that conditioning
\hat{\varphi}_{ji}^{n}
on
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}
it is equivalent to conditioning on
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
by the i.i.d. structure of
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}
and that
\hat{\varphi}_{ji}^{n}
only depends on
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
. First, we define for all
j\not=i
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
m\leq n
the following conditional expectation regression error
\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}:=\{\varphi_{ji}(X_{m,j})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}^{n}(X_{m,j})\}
.
Furthermore, for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
m\leq n
define

\displaystyle\Psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}}):=\begin{bmatrix}\hat{R}_{nm}^{2}-\delta_{n}^{2}-\mu\\
\hat{V}_{m}-\nu\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{p^{2}},

where only
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
(containing the first
n
coordinates of
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}
) is used, and

\displaystyle\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}}).

Note that the desired conclusion of
Equation

C.17
follows by verifying condition (a), (b) and (c) of
Lemma

C.1
. First, we show (a), the conditional mean zero condition. To that end, note that for any
i\in\{1,\ldots,p\}
and
j\in\{1,\ldots,p\}\setminus\{i\}
it holds that

\displaystyle\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}

\displaystyle=(X_{m,i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{m,j})+\varphi_{ji}(X_{m,j})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}^{n}(X_{m,j}))^{2}

\displaystyle=(R_{m,ji}+\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji})^{2}

\displaystyle=R_{m,ji}^{2}+\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}^{2}+2R_{m,ji}\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}.

Hence, we have that

\displaystyle\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}-\mu_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2}

\displaystyle=(R_{m,ji}^{2}-\mu_{ji})+(\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}^{2}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2})+2R_{m,ji}\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}.

(C.18)

The terms of
Equation

C.18
are mean zero conditionally on
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}
, since
\mathbb{E}[R_{m,ji}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]=\mathbb{E}[R_{m,ji}^{2}]=\mu_{ji}
,
\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]=\delta_{n,ji}^{2}
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[R_{m,ji}\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[R_{m,ji}\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}},X_{m,j}]|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[X_{m,i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{m,j})|\tilde{\mathbf{X}},X_{m,j}]\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\mathbb{E}[X_{m,i}|X_{m,j}]-\varphi_{ji}(X_{m,j}))\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle=0,

as
\varphi_{ji}(X_{m,j})=\mathbb{E}[X_{m,i}|X_{m,j}]
almost surely. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{m,i}^{2}-\mathrm{Var}(X_{i})|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]=\mathbb{E}[X_{m,i}^{2}]-\mathrm{Var}(X_{i})=0.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{bmatrix}\hat{R}_{nm}^{2}-\delta_{n}^{2}-\mu\\
\hat{V}_{m}-\nu\end{bmatrix}\bigg|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right]=0,

almost surely. With respect to (b), convergence of the sum of variances, we have, by assumption, that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{n}:=\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{n,R}&\Sigma_{n,RV}\\
\Sigma_{n,RV}^{\intercal}&\Sigma_{n,V}\end{bmatrix}:=\mathrm{Var}\left(\Psi_{n}(X_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\Sigma,

(C.19)

where
\Sigma
is a positive semi-definite matrix. Furthermore, we have that

(X_{m})_{m\geq 1}
is an i.i.d. sequence independent of
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{n}\mathrm{Var}(\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})|\tilde{\mathbf{X}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{n}\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{Var}(\Psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})|\tilde{\mathbf{X}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{n}\frac{1}{n}\Sigma_{n}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{n}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\Sigma.

Finally, we show that condition (c), a conditional Lindeberg-Feller condition, is fulfilled. To this end, note that with
\varepsilon:=\xi/2>0
we have that

\displaystyle\,\,\mathbb{E}\left[\|\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})\|^{2+\varepsilon}_{2}\big|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{R}_{nm}^{2}-\delta_{n}^{2}-\mu\\
\hat{V}_{m}-\nu\end{bmatrix}\right\|_{2}^{2+\varepsilon}\bigg|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right]

(C.20)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{\frac{2+\varepsilon}{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\hat{R}_{nm}^{2}-\delta_{n}^{2}-\mu\\
\hat{V}_{m}-\nu\end{bmatrix}\right\|_{2}^{2+\varepsilon}\bigg|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{\frac{2+\varepsilon}{2}}}2^{(\frac{2+\varepsilon}{2}-1)}\bigg(\sum_{i\not=j}\mathbb{E}\left[|\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}-\mu_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2}|^{2+\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}|X_{m,i}^{2}-\mathrm{Var}(X_{i})|^{2+\varepsilon}\bigg),

(C.21)

by the cr inequality. We now realize that
the second factor of
Equation

C.21
is stochastically bounded. To see this, note that for any
j\not=i
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}-\mu_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2}|^{2+\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{1+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{E}[|\hat{R}_{nm,ji}|^{4+2\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]+\mu_{ji}^{2+\varepsilon}+\mathbb{E}[|\delta_{n,ji}^{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}})|^{2+\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]).

(C.22)

The first term of the upper bound in
Equation

C.22
is
O_{p}(1)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\hat{R}_{nm,ji}|^{4+2\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[|X_{m,i}-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}^{n}(X_{m,j})|^{4+2\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{3+2\varepsilon}(\mathbb{E}|X_{m,i}-\varphi_{ji}(X_{m,j})|^{4+2\varepsilon}+\mathbb{E}[|\varphi_{ji}(X_{m,i})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}^{n}(X_{m,j})|^{4+2\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}])

\displaystyle=2^{3+2\varepsilon}(\mathbb{E}[|R_{m,ji}|^{4+\xi}]+\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|^{4+\xi}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}])=O_{p}(1),

as
\mathbb{E}\|X\|_{2}^{4+\xi}<\infty
and
\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|^{4+\xi}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]=O_{p}(1)
. This holds because
R_{m,ji}=\{X_{m,i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{m,i}|X_{m,j}]\}
and both terms are in
\mathcal{L}^{4+\xi}(P)
if
X_{m,i}\in\mathcal{L}^{4+\xi}(P)
which is guaranteed as
\mathbb{E}\|X\|_{2}^{4+\xi}<\infty
. For the third term in the upper bound of
Equation

C.22
, we note that by the conditional Jensen’s inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\delta_{n,ji}^{2}|^{2+\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]\leq\mathbb{E}[|\varphi_{ji}(X_{m,i})-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}^{n}(X_{m,j})|^{4+2\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]=\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|^{4+\xi}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]=O_{p}(1),

by assumption. Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})\|^{2+\varepsilon}_{2}\big|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right]\leq\frac{n}{n^{\frac{2+\varepsilon}{2}}}O_{p}(1)=n^{-\varepsilon/2}O_{p}(1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}0,

proving the conditional Lindeberg-Feller condition. By
Lemma

C.1
it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\psi_{n}(X_{m},\tilde{\mathbf{X}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma).

Now it only remains to prove that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma_{n}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,

or, equivalently, that each entry converges to zero in probability. For example, for the entries of the first block matrix with
j\not=i
and
l\not=r
we prove that

\displaystyle|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,R,ji,lr}-\Sigma_{n,R,ji,lr}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

Now note that the observable estimated covariance matrix entry is given by

\displaystyle\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,R,ji,lr}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}-\hat{\mu}_{n,ji}\hat{\mu}_{n,lr},

while the unobservable conditional covariance matrix is given by

\displaystyle\Sigma_{n,R,ji,lr}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}-\mu_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2})(\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}-\mu_{lr}-\delta_{n,lr}^{2})|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]-(\mu_{ji}+\delta_{n,ji}^{2})(\mu_{lr}+\delta_{n,lr}^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]-\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}],

where we have used that
\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]=\mu_{ji}+\delta_{n,ji}^{2}
; see
Equation

C.18
and its discussion. Note that the second term of the covariance matrix estimator expands to

\displaystyle\hat{\mu}_{n,ji}\hat{\mu}_{n,lr}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}]\right)\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}]\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}]\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}],

Thus

\displaystyle\,|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,R,ji,lr}-\Sigma_{n,R,ji,lr}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}(\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}])

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]\right)\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}(\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]-\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}])

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}(\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]-\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]\mathbb{E}[\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}])\bigg|.

(C.23)

Each of these terms tends to zero in probability by
Lemma

C.3
. For example, for the first term of
Equation

C.23
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}|^{1+\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right]=O_{p}(1),

for some
\varepsilon>0
. Fix
\varepsilon=\xi/4
and note, by the cr-inequality, that

\displaystyle\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}

\displaystyle=(X_{m,i}-\hat{\varphi}_{ji}^{n}(X_{m,j}))^{2}(X_{m,r}-\hat{\varphi}_{lr}^{n}(X_{m,l}))^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4(R_{m,ji}^{2}+\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}^{2})(R_{m,lr}^{2}+\hat{\delta}_{nm,lr}^{2}).

Thus, by the cr-inequality and the conditional Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we have, with
c=4^{1+\varepsilon}2^{2\varepsilon}
, that

\displaystyle c^{-1}\mathbb{E}[|\hat{R}_{nm,ji}^{2}\hat{R}_{nm,lr}^{2}|^{1+\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,c^{-1}4^{1+\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}[|R_{m,ji}^{2}+\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}^{2}|^{1+\varepsilon}|R_{m,lr}^{2}+\hat{\delta}_{nm,lr}^{2}|^{1+\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{E}[(|R_{m,ji}|^{2+2\varepsilon}+|\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|^{2+2\varepsilon})(|R_{m,lr}|^{2+2\varepsilon}+|\hat{\delta}_{nm,lr}|^{2+2\varepsilon})|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{E}[|R_{m,ji}|^{2+2\varepsilon}|R_{m,lr}|^{2+2\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]+\mathbb{E}[|R_{m,ji}|^{2+2\varepsilon}|\hat{\delta}_{nm,lr}|^{2+2\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|^{2+2\varepsilon}|R_{m,lr}|^{2+2\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]+\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|^{2+2\varepsilon}|\hat{\delta}_{nm,lr}|^{2+2\varepsilon}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{E}[|R_{m,ji}|^{4+\xi}]\mathbb{E}[|R_{m,lr}|^{4+\xi}]+\mathbb{E}[|R_{m,ji}|^{4+\xi}]\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\delta}_{nm,lr}|^{4+\xi}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|^{4+\xi}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]\mathbb{E}[|R_{m,lr}|^{4+\xi}]+\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|^{4+\xi}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\delta}_{nm,lr}|^{4+\xi}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,O_{p}(1),

as
\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|^{4+\xi}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]=O_{p}(1)
for all
j\not=i
by assumption and
\mathbb{E}[|R_{m,ji}|^{4+\xi}]<\infty
since
\mathbb{E}\|X\|_{2}^{4+\xi}<\infty
.

Similar arguments show convergence in probability of the entries in the other block submatrices of
\widehat{\Sigma}_{n}
less
\Sigma_{n}
, yielding the desired conclusion.

\square

Proof of
Theorem

4.3
:

We prove the theorem under the simplifying assumption that
\mathbb{E}[X]=0
for which we can simplify the variance estimator by
\hat{V}_{m,i}:=X_{m,i}^{2}
and
\hat{\nu}_{n,i}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\hat{V}_{m,i}
for all
1\leq i\leq p
.

First, note (using the notation introduced in
Lemma

C.4
) that
\hat{M}_{1}=\{\hat{R}_{n1,ji}^{2}\}_{j\not=i}
,
\hat{\mu}=\hat{\mu}_{n}
,
\hat{\nu}=\hat{\nu}_{n}
and
\widehat{\Sigma}=\widehat{\Sigma}_{n}
. The conditional mean of
\hat{M}_{1}
given
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\hat{M}_{1}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]=\mathbb{E}[\{\hat{R}_{n1,ji}^{2}\}_{j\not=i}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]=\mu+\delta_{n}^{2},

see
Equation

C.18
. Similarly we have that
\mathbb{E}[\hat{V}_{1}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]=\mathbb{E}[\hat{V}_{1}]=\nu
. Subtracting a constant (conditional on
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
) does not change the conditional variance, hence

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\hat{R}_{n1}^{2}-\delta_{n}^{2}-\mu\\
\hat{V}_{1}-\nu\end{bmatrix}\bigg|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right)=\mathrm{Var}\left((\hat{M}_{1}^{\intercal},\hat{V}_{1}^{\intercal})^{\intercal}\bigg|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\Sigma.

\Sigma
is constant and positive semi-definite with strictly positive diagonal. As such, the conditions of
Lemma

C.4
is satisfied, which yields that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{R}_{nm}^{2}-\delta_{n}^{2}-\mu\\
\hat{V}_{m}-\nu\end{bmatrix}=\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\mu}-\delta_{n}^{2}-\mu\\
\hat{\nu}-\nu\end{bmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma),

(C.24)

and that

\displaystyle\widehat{\Sigma}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\Sigma}_{M}&\widehat{\Sigma}_{MV}\\
\widehat{\Sigma}_{MV}^{\intercal}&\widehat{\Sigma}_{V}\end{bmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Sigma=:\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{M}&\Sigma_{MV}\\
\Sigma_{MV}^{\intercal}&\Sigma_{V}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{p^{2}\times p^{2}}.

For any
j\not=i
we denote

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{ji}=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\hat{\mu}_{ji}}{\hat{\nu}_{i}}\right),\quad\tilde{w}_{ji}=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2}}{\hat{\nu}_{i}}\right),\quad w_{ji}=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\mu_{ji}}{\nu_{i}}\right),

where the latter is a shorthand notation for the Gaussian edge weight
w_{\mathrm{G}}(j\to i)
. Fix
\alpha\in(0,1)
. First, consider
(j\to i)\in\mathcal{E}
and note that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\mu_{ji}\\
\hat{\nu}_{i}-\nu_{i}\end{bmatrix}-\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2}-\mu_{ji}\\
\hat{\nu}_{i}-\nu_{i}\end{bmatrix}\right)=\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\delta_{n,ji}^{2}\\
0\end{bmatrix}=\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}]\\
0\end{bmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}0,

(C.25)

by assumption (iv).
Hence,
Equation

C.24
,
Equation

C.25
and the delta method yields that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\hat{w}_{ji}-w_{ji}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\left(\log\left(\frac{\hat{\mu}_{ji}}{\hat{\nu}_{i}}\right)-\log\left(\frac{\mu_{ji}}{\nu_{i}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}(\log(\hat{\mu}_{ji})-\log(\mu_{ji})-\log(\hat{\nu}_{i})+\log(\nu_{i}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{ji}^{2}),

where

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{ji}^{2}:=\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}}{\hat{\mu}_{ji}^{2}}+\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{V,i}}{\hat{\nu}_{i}^{2}}-2\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{MV,ji,i}}{\hat{\mu}_{ji}\hat{\nu}_{i}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sigma_{ji}^{2}:=\frac{\Sigma_{M,ji}}{\mu_{ji}^{2}}+\frac{\Sigma_{V,i}}{\nu_{i}^{2}}-2\frac{\Sigma_{MV,ji,i}}{\mu_{ji}\nu_{i}}\geq 0.

Here
\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}
and
\widehat{\Sigma}_{V,i}
and their limits use a shorthand notation that denote the corresponding diagonal element, e.g.,
\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}:=\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji,ji}
.

An asymptotically valid marginal confidence interval for
w_{ji}
with level
\alpha
is, by virtue of the above convergence in distribution, given by

\hat{w}_{ji}\pm\hat{\sigma}_{ji}\frac{q(1-\frac{\alpha}{2})}{2\sqrt{n}},

where
q(1-\frac{\alpha}{2})
is the
1-\alpha/2
quantile of the standard normal distribution. That is,

P\left(\hat{w}_{ji}-\hat{\sigma}_{ji}\frac{q(1-\frac{\alpha}{2})}{2\sqrt{n}}\leq w_{ji}\leq\hat{w}_{ji}+\hat{\sigma}_{ji}\frac{q(1-\frac{\alpha}{2})}{2\sqrt{n}}\right)\longrightarrow_{n}1-\alpha.

On the other hand, for any
(j\to i)\not\in\mathcal{E}
we have, by similar arguments, except that no assumption guarantees that
\sqrt{n}\delta_{n,ji}^{2}
vanishes, that

\displaystyle P\left(\tilde{w}_{ji}-\tilde{\sigma}_{ji}\frac{q(1-\frac{\alpha}{2})}{2\sqrt{n}}\leq w_{ji}\leq\tilde{w}_{ji}+\tilde{\sigma}_{ji}\frac{q(1-\frac{\alpha}{2})}{2\sqrt{n}}\right)\longrightarrow_{n}1-\alpha,

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}_{ji}^{2}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}}{(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2})^{2}}+\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{V,i}}{\hat{\nu}_{i}^{2}}-2\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{MV,ji,i}}{(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2})\hat{\nu}_{i}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sigma_{ji}^{2}:=\frac{\Sigma_{M,ji}}{\mu_{ji}^{2}}+\frac{\Sigma_{V,i}}{\nu_{i}^{2}}-2\frac{\Sigma_{MV,ji,i}}{\mu_{ji}\nu_{i}}\geq 0,

by the convergence in
Equation

C.24
.
Note that
\tilde{\sigma}_{ji}^{2}
is not observable since
\delta_{n,ji}^{2}
is not observable.
Now define

\displaystyle\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji},\,\hat{l}_{\alpha,ji}

\displaystyle:=\hat{w}_{ji}\pm\hat{\sigma}_{ji}\frac{q\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2p(p-1)}\right)}{2\sqrt{n}},

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{\alpha,ji},\,\tilde{l}_{\alpha,ji}

\displaystyle:=\tilde{w}_{ji}\pm\tilde{\sigma}_{ji}\frac{q\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2p(p-1)}\right)}{2\sqrt{n}}.

Thus, we have the following Bonferroni corrected simultaneous confidence interval for the Gaussian edge weights

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}P\left(\bigcap_{(j\to i)\in\mathcal{E}}\left(w_{ji}\in\left[\hat{l}_{\alpha,ji},\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji}\right]\right)\bigcap_{j\to i\not\in\mathcal{E}}\left(w_{ji}\in\left[\tilde{l}_{\alpha,ji},\tilde{u}_{\alpha,ji}\right]\right)\right)\geq 1-\alpha.

The above confidence region has the correct asymptotic level, but it is infeasible to compute in that
\tilde{w}_{ji}
,
\tilde{\sigma}_{ji}
and
\mathcal{E}
are not directly observable from data.
Furthermore, define

\displaystyle C(\hat{l}_{\alpha},\tilde{l}_{\alpha},\hat{u}_{\alpha},\tilde{u}_{\alpha}):=\bigg\{\argmin_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}=(V,\tilde{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathcal{T}_{p}}\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}}w_{ji}^{\prime}:

\displaystyle\forall(j\to i)\in\mathcal{E},w_{ji}^{\prime}\in[\hat{l}_{\alpha,ji},\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji}],

\displaystyle\forall(j\to i)\not\in\mathcal{E},w_{ji}^{\prime}\in[\tilde{l}_{\alpha,ji},\tilde{u}_{\alpha,ji}]\bigg\},

and note that this is an unobservable confidence region for the causal graph. That is,

\displaystyle\,\liminf_{n\to\infty}P(\mathcal{G}\in C(\hat{l}_{\alpha},\tilde{l}_{\alpha},\hat{u}_{\alpha},\tilde{u}_{\alpha}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\,\liminf_{n\to\infty}P\left(\bigcap_{(j\to i)\in\mathcal{E}}(w_{ji}\in[\hat{l}_{\alpha,ji},\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji}])\bigcap_{(j\to i)\not\in\mathcal{E}}(w_{ji}\in[\tilde{l}_{\alpha,ji},\tilde{u}_{\alpha,ji}])\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\,1-\alpha.

Our proposed confidence region has the form

\displaystyle\hat{C}:=C(\hat{l}_{\alpha},\hat{u}_{\alpha}):=\bigg\{\argmin_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}=(V,\tilde{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathcal{T}_{p}}\sum_{(j\to i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}}w_{ji}^{\prime}:

\displaystyle\forall j\not=i,w_{ji}^{\prime}\in[\hat{l}_{\alpha,ji},\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji}]\bigg\},

which corresponds to the biased but computable confidence region

\displaystyle\prod_{j\not=i}[\hat{l}_{\alpha,ji},\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji}]

\displaystyle=\prod_{j\not=i}\left[\hat{w}_{ji}\pm\hat{\sigma}_{ji}\frac{q\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2p(p-1)}\right)}{2\sqrt{n}}\right].

for the Gaussian edge weights, where the product is over all combinations of possible edges
1\leq j\not=i\leq p
. The biased confidence region
\prod_{j\not=i}[\hat{l}_{\alpha,ji},\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji}]
does not necessarily contain the population Gaussian edge weights with a probability of at least
1-\alpha
in the large sample limit. However, it can be used to construct a conservative confidence region for the causal graph.
To see this, note that by further penalizing the wrong (non-causal) edge weights, the causal graph still yields the minimum edge weight spanning directed tree. Hence,

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}P(\mathcal{G}\in C(\hat{l}_{\alpha},\hat{u}_{\alpha}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}P\left(\bigcap_{(j\to i)\in\mathcal{E}}(w_{ji}\in[\hat{l}_{\alpha,ji},\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji}])\bigcap_{(j\to i)\not\in\mathcal{E}}(w_{ji}\in[\tilde{l}_{\alpha,ji},\tilde{u}_{\alpha,ji}])\bigcap_{(j\to i)\not\in\mathcal{E}}(\tilde{u}_{\alpha,ji}\leq\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji})\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-\alpha,

as
P\left(\tilde{u}_{\alpha,ji}\leq\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji}\right)\to_{n}1
for all
(j\to i)\not\in\mathcal{E}
by
Lemma

C.5
below.

\square

Lemma C.5
.

Suppose that the assumptions of
Lemma

C.4
hold. It holds that

\displaystyle\forall(j\to i)\not\in\mathcal{E},\forall\alpha\in(0,1):P\left(\tilde{u}_{\alpha,ji}\leq\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji}\right)\to_{n}1.

Proof of
Lemma

C.5
:

Fix any
(j\to i)\not\in\mathcal{E}
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
and note that we want to show that

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{\alpha,ji}\leq\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\tilde{w}_{ji}+c\frac{\tilde{\sigma}_{ji}}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\hat{w}_{ji}+c\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{ji}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle 0\leq\log\left(\hat{\mu}_{ji}\right)+c\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{ji}}{\sqrt{n}}-\log\left(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2}\right)-c\frac{\tilde{\sigma}_{ji}}{\sqrt{n}}

holds with probability converging to one, where
c
is a strictly positive constant. It suffices to show that an even smaller quantity is non-negative with probability converging to one. That is, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle 0\leq\log\left(\hat{\mu}_{ji}\right)+c\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{ji}}{\sqrt{n}}-\log\left(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2}\right)-c\frac{\tilde{\sigma}_{ji}^{*}}{\sqrt{n}},

with increasing probability, where

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}_{ji}^{*}:=\sqrt{\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}}{(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2})^{2}}+\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{V,i}}{\hat{\nu}_{i}^{2}}+2\frac{|\widehat{\Sigma}_{MV,ji,i}|}{(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2})\hat{\nu}_{i}}}\geq\tilde{\sigma}_{ji},

with
P(\tilde{\sigma}_{ji}^{*}>0)\to_{n}1
.
Let
d_{n}(t):[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
denote the random function given by

\displaystyle d_{n}(t):=

\displaystyle\log\left(\hat{\mu}_{ji}\right)+c\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{ji}}{\sqrt{n}}-\log\left(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-t\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}}{(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-t)^{2}}+\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{V,i}}{\hat{\nu}_{i}^{2}}+2\frac{|\widehat{\Sigma}_{MV,ji,i}|}{(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-t)\hat{\nu}_{i}}}.

It holds that
d_{n}(0)=0
surely, so by the mean value theorem, the desired conclusion holds if it with probability one (as
n
tends to infinity) holds, for all
t\in[0,\delta_{n,ji}^{2}]
, that
d_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq 0
.

Now fix
\eta>0
and choose
M_{\eta},\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{5}>0
such that the constant lower bounds in the following inequalities are strictly positive

\displaystyle\Omega_{n}(1):

\displaystyle=(\hat{\mu}_{ji}\leq M_{\eta}),

\displaystyle\Omega_{n}(2):

\displaystyle=(\Sigma_{M,ji}-\varepsilon_{1}\leq\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}\leq\Sigma_{M,ji}+\varepsilon_{1}),

\displaystyle\Omega_{n}(3):

\displaystyle=(\Sigma_{V,i}-\varepsilon_{2}\leq\widehat{\Sigma}_{V,i}\leq\Sigma_{V,i}+\varepsilon_{2}),

\displaystyle\Omega_{n}(4):

\displaystyle=(0\leq|\widehat{\Sigma}_{MV,ji,i}|\leq|\Sigma_{MV,ji,i}|+\varepsilon_{3}),

\displaystyle\Omega_{n}(5):

\displaystyle=(\mu_{ji}-\varepsilon_{4}\leq\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2}\leq\mu_{ji}+\varepsilon_{4}),

\displaystyle\Omega_{n}(6):

\displaystyle=(\nu_{i}-\varepsilon_{5}\leq\hat{\nu}_{i}\leq\nu_{i}+\varepsilon_{5}),

and
\liminf_{n\to\infty}P(\Omega_{n}(1))>1-\eta
.
This is possible as
\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\mu_{ji}>0
and

\displaystyle\delta_{n,ji}^{2}=E[|\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|^{2}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle=E[|\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|^{\frac{4+\xi}{2+\xi/2}}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]

\displaystyle\leq E[|\hat{\delta}_{nm,ji}|^{4+\xi}|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}]^{\frac{1}{2+\xi/2}}=O_{p}(1),

by the conditional Jensen’s inequality and concavity of
[0,\infty)\ni x\mapsto x^{\frac{1}{2+\xi/2}}
, which implies that
\hat{\mu}_{ji}=(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\hat{\delta}_{n,ji}^{2}-\mu_{ji})+(\hat{\delta}_{n,ji}^{2}+\mu_{ji})=o_{p}(1)+O_{p}(1)=O_{p}(1)
. Furthermore, as

\displaystyle\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\Sigma_{M,ji}>0,\quad\widehat{\Sigma}_{V,i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\Sigma_{V,i}>0,

\displaystyle|\widehat{\Sigma}_{MV,ji,i}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}|\Sigma_{MV,ji,i}|\geq 0,\quad\hat{\nu}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{n}\nu_{i}>0,

it holds that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}P\left(\bigcup_{1\leq k\leq 6}\Omega_{n}(k)^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq k\leq 6}\limsup_{n\to\infty}P(\Omega_{n}(k)^{c})

\displaystyle=\limsup_{n\to\infty}P(\Omega_{n}(1)^{c})\leq\eta.

Here we used that the diagonal elements of the limit covariance matrix are assumed strictly positive. That
\mu_{ji},\nu_{i}>0
follows from the fact that
X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}|X_{j}]
is assumed to have a density (w.r.t. Lebesgue measure) and that the variables are non-degenerate
\nu_{i}=\text{Var}(X_{i})>0
.
Thus, we have that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}P\left(\bigcap_{1\leq k\leq 6}\Omega_{n}(k)\right)>1-\eta.

Now consider a fixed
\omega\in\bigcap_{1\leq k\leq 6}\Omega_{n}(k)
and note that with
g_{n}:[0,\delta_{n,ji}^{2}]\to\mathbb{R}
given by
g_{n}(t)=\hat{\mu}_{ji}-t
we have that
g_{n}
is decreasing and that

\displaystyle g_{n}([0,\delta_{n,ji}^{2}])\subseteq[\mu_{ji}-\varepsilon_{4},\hat{\mu}_{ji}]\subseteq(0,M_{\eta}]

We have for any
t\in[0,\delta_{n,ji}^{2}]
that

\displaystyle d_{n}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\hat{\mu}_{ji}-t}-\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}}{(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-t)^{2}}+\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{V,i}}{\hat{\nu}_{i}^{2}}+\frac{2|\widehat{\Sigma}_{MV,ji,i}|}{(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-t)\hat{\nu}_{i}}\right)^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}}{(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-t)^{3}}+\frac{|\widehat{\Sigma}_{MV,ji,i}|}{(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-t)^{2}\hat{\nu}_{i}}\right),

hence,

\displaystyle d_{n}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\hat{\mu}_{ji}-t}-\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}}{g_{n}(t)^{2}}+\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{V,i}}{\hat{\nu}_{i}^{2}}+\frac{2|\widehat{\Sigma}_{MV,ji,i}|}{g_{n}(t)\hat{\nu}_{i}}\right)^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}}{g_{n}(t)^{3}}+\frac{|\widehat{\Sigma}_{MV,ji,i}|}{g_{n}(t)^{2}\hat{\nu}_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\hat{\mu}_{ji}}-\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}}{\hat{\mu}_{ji}^{2}}+\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{V,i}}{\hat{\nu}_{i}^{2}}\right)^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\frac{\widehat{\Sigma}_{M,ji}}{(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2})^{3}}+\frac{|\widehat{\Sigma}_{MV,ji,i}|}{(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2})^{2}\hat{\nu}_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{M_{\eta}}-\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\frac{\Sigma_{M,ji}-\varepsilon_{1}}{M_{\eta}^{2}}+\frac{\Sigma_{V,i}-\varepsilon_{2}}{(\nu_{i}+\varepsilon_{5})^{2}}\right)^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\frac{\Sigma_{M,ji}+\varepsilon_{1}}{(\mu_{ji}-\varepsilon_{4})^{3}}+\frac{|\Sigma_{MV,ji,i}|+\varepsilon_{3}}{(\mu_{ji}-\varepsilon_{4})^{2}(\nu_{i}-\varepsilon_{5})}\right)

\displaystyle=:\frac{1}{M_{\eta}}-\frac{C_{M_{\eta},\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{4},\varepsilon_{5}}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\geq 0,

for
n\geq(C_{M_{\eta},\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{4},\varepsilon_{5}}M_{\eta})^{2}
. We conclude that for
n\geq(C_{M_{\eta},\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{4},\varepsilon_{5}}M_{\eta})^{2}

\displaystyle P\left(\tilde{u}_{\alpha,ji}\leq\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji}\right)

\displaystyle=P\left(0\leq\log\left(\hat{\mu}_{ji}\right)+c\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{ji}}{\sqrt{n}}-\log\left(\hat{\mu}_{ji}-\delta_{n,ji}^{2}\right)-c\frac{\tilde{\sigma}_{ji}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq P\left(\forall t\in[0,\delta_{n,ji}^{2}]:d_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq 0\right)

\displaystyle\geq P\left(\bigcap_{1\leq k\leq 6}\Omega_{n}(k)\right).

Hence,

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}P\left(\tilde{u}_{\alpha,ji}\leq\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji}\right)\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}P\left(\bigcap_{1\leq k\leq 6}\Omega_{n}(k)\right)\geq 1-\eta,

and as
\eta>0
was chosen arbitrarily, we have the desired conclusion

\displaystyle P\left(\tilde{u}_{\alpha,ji}\leq\hat{u}_{\alpha,ji}\right)\to 1.

\square

Proof of
Theorem

4.4
:

Consider a collection of arbitrary and possibly data-dependent substructures
\mathcal{R}_{1},\mathcal{R}_{2},...
and level
\alpha\in(0,1)
. First, we note that the score associated with two sets of edge weights
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
is weakly monotone, that is,
s(w_{1})\leq s(w_{2})
if

w_{1}
and
w_{2}
satisfy the component-wise partial ordering
w_{1}\leq w_{2}
. Furthermore, the restricted score function
w\mapsto s_{\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{R})}(w)
is also weakly monotone for any set of restrictions
\mathcal{R}
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose that the null hypothesis

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{0}(\mathcal{R}_{k}):\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{k}}\setminus\mathcal{E}=\emptyset,\;\mathcal{E}\setminus\mathcal{E}^{\text{miss}}_{\mathcal{R}_{k}}=\emptyset,\;r_{k}=\mathrm{rt}(\mathcal{G}),

corresponding to the restriction
\mathcal{R}_{k}=(\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{k}},\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{k}}^{\text{miss}},r_{k})
is true.

If there is a graph in
\hat{C}:=\hat{C}(\hat{l}_{\alpha},\hat{u}_{\alpha})
satisfying the restrictions imposed by the substructure
\mathcal{R}_{k}
, then there exist
\hat{l}_{\alpha}\leq w^{\prime}\leq\hat{u}_{\alpha}
such that
s(w^{\prime})
attains its minimum value in a graph satisfying
\mathcal{R}_{k}
. Penalizing (or removing) edges that are not present in the minimum edge weight directed tree does not affect the score of the minimum edge weigh directed tree. Hence, it holds that

s_{\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{R}_{k})}(w^{\prime})=s(w^{\prime}).

Monotonicity of
s_{\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{R}_{k})}
and
s
in the edge weights imply that

s_{\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{R}_{k})}(\hat{l}_{\alpha})\leq s_{\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{R}_{k})}(w^{\prime})=s(w^{\prime})\leq s(\hat{u}_{\alpha}).

Hence,
s_{\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{R}_{k})}(\hat{l}_{\alpha})>s(\hat{u}_{\alpha})
entails that no graph in
\hat{C}
satisfies the restrictions of
\mathcal{R}_{k}
. (This is a slightly conservative criterion as
s_{\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{R}_{k})}(\hat{l}_{\alpha})\leq s(\hat{u}_{\alpha})
does not necessarily guarantee that a graph in
\hat{C}
satisfies the restrictions of
\mathcal{R}_{k}
.)

Therefore, if
\psi_{\mathcal{R}_{k}}=1
, then we know that there is no graph in
\hat{C}
satisfying the restrictions of
\mathcal{R}_{k}
. As the causal graph
\mathcal{G}
satisfies the restriction
\mathcal{R}_{k}
we conclude that
\mathcal{G}
is not contained in
\hat{C}
. Thus for any true
\mathcal{R}_{k}
we have that

\displaystyle(\psi_{\mathcal{R}_{k}}=1)\subseteq(\mathcal{G}\not\in\hat{C}).

Since this holds for any true
\mathcal{R}_{k}
, the conclusion follows by noting that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}P\left(\bigcup_{k:\mathcal{H}_{0}(\mathcal{R}_{k})\text{ is true}}\{\psi_{\mathcal{R}_{k}}=1\}\right)\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}P(\mathcal{G}\not\in\hat{C})\leq\alpha,

where we used
Theorem

4.3
.

\square

C.50.4
Proofs of Section
4.22

C.50.4.1
Proofs of first results in Section
4.22

Proof of
Lemma

4.3
:

As conditioning reduces entropy we always have that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{CE}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}},i)=h(X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}})
