\displaystyle=h(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}]|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}})

\displaystyle\leq h(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}])

\displaystyle=\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}},i).

Furthermore, note that when conditioning we ‘throw out’ dependence information captured by the mutual information
I(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}];X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}})
, which is zero if and only if
X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}]\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}
. This is especially the case for the true graph, i.e.,
X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}}({i})}}]\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}}({i})}}
, implying that
\ell_{\mathrm{CE}}(\mathcal{G},i)=\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G},i)
. Consequently, we have that the local conditional entropy score gap lower bounds the local entropy score gap,

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{CE}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}},i)-\ell_{\mathrm{CE}}(\mathcal{G},i)\leq\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}},i)-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G},i).

Furthermore, from the arguments in the proof of
Lemma

4.2
we have that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}},i)

\displaystyle=\inf_{\tilde{N}_{i}\sim P_{\tilde{N}_{i}}\in\mathcal{P}}h\left(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}\right],\tilde{N}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\tilde{N}_{i}\sim P_{\tilde{N}_{i}}\in\mathcal{P}_{G}}h\left(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}({i})}}\right],\tilde{N}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}},i)+\log(\sqrt{2\pi e}).

If
X
is generated by a Gaussian noise model, i.e., with generating SCM
\theta=(\mathcal{G},(f_{i}),P_{N})
with
P_{N}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{G}}^{p}
, then
\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G},i)=h(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}|X_{{\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}}({i})}}])=h(N_{i})=\log(\sqrt{2\pi e}\sigma_{i})=\log(\sqrt{2\pi e})+\frac{1}{2}\log(\mathbb{E}[N_{i}^{2}])=\log(\sqrt{2\pi e})+l_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G},i)
, in which case the local entropy score gap lower bounds the local Gaussian score gap

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}},i)-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G},i)\leq\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}},i)-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G},i).

\square

Proof of
Lemma

4.4
:

Note that
\mathbb{E}[Y|X]=\mathbb{E}[f(X)+N_{Y}|X]=f(X)+\mathbb{E}[N_{Y}|X]=f(X)+\mathbb{E}[N_{Y}]
, since
N_{Y}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}N_{X}=X
. Hence, the score difference can be written as

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})=

\displaystyle\,\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}},X)-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G},X)+\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}},Y)-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G},Y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,h(X-\mathbb{E}(X|Y))-h(X)+h(Y)-h(Y-E(Y|X))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,h(X-\mathbb{E}(X|Y))-h(X)+h(Y)-h(N_{Y}+\mathbb{E}[N_{Y}])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,h(X-\mathbb{E}(X|Y))-h(X)+h(Y)-h(N_{Y}),

as the differential entropy is translation invariant.
Now note that as
N_{Y}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}N_{X}
it holds that
N_{Y}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}f(X)
, so conditioning on
f(X)
yields that

\displaystyle h(Y)

\displaystyle=h(Y|f(X))+I(Y;f(X))

\displaystyle=h(f(X)+N_{Y}|f(X))+I(Y;f(X))

\displaystyle=h(N_{Y})+I(Y;f(X)).

Similarly, conditioning on
X
yields that

\displaystyle h(Y)

\displaystyle=h(Y|X)+I(Y;X)

\displaystyle=h(N_{Y})+I(Y;X),

which proves that

\displaystyle I(Y;f(X))=I(Y;X).

This equality is normally derived by restricting
f
to be bijective, but here it holds regardless by the structural assignment form, as
Y
only depends on
X
through
f(X)
. Furthermore, we have that

\displaystyle h(X-\mathbb{E}[X|Y])

\displaystyle=I(X-\mathbb{E}[X|Y];Y)+h(X-\mathbb{E}[X|Y]|Y)

\displaystyle=I(X-\mathbb{E}[X|Y];Y)+h(X|Y).

Hence,

\displaystyle h(X-\mathbb{E}[X|Y])-h(X)

\displaystyle=I(X-\mathbb{E}[X|Y];Y)+h(X|Y)-h(X)

\displaystyle=I(X-\mathbb{E}[X|Y];Y)-I(Y;X).

Thus

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle=h(X-\mathbb{E}[X|Y])-h(X)+h(Y)-h(N_{Y})

\displaystyle=I(X-\mathbb{E}[X|Y];Y)-I(Y;X)+h(N_{Y})+I(Y;f(X))-h(N_{Y})

\displaystyle=I(X-\mathbb{E}[X|Y];Y)-I(Y;X)+I(Y;f(X))

\displaystyle=I(X-\mathbb{E}[X|Y];Y),

proving the claim.

\square

Proof of
Proposition

4.2
:

As the conditional mean
\mathbb{E}[X|Y]
vanishes, we have that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle=I(X-\mathbb{E}(X|Y);Y)

\displaystyle=I(X;Y)

\displaystyle=I(Y;X)

\displaystyle=I(Y;f(X)),

where the last equality was derived in the proof of
Lemma

4.4
.
Now let
f(X)^{\mathrm{G}}
and
N_{Y}^{\mathrm{G}}
be independent normal distributed random variables with the same mean and variance as
f(X)
and
N_{Y}
. That is,
f(X)^{\mathrm{G}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{E}[f(X)],\mathrm{Var}(f(X)))
,
N_{Y}^{\mathrm{G}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{E}[N_{Y}],\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y}))
with
N_{Y}^{\mathrm{G}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}f(X)^{\mathrm{G}}
such that
f(X)^{\mathrm{G}}+N_{Y}^{\mathrm{G}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{E}[f(X)]+\mathbb{E}[N_{Y}],\mathrm{Var}(f(X))+\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y}))
.

(a)

If
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(f(X)\|f(X)^{\mathrm{G}})\leq D_{\mathrm{KL}}(N_{Y}\|N_{Y}^{\mathrm{G}})
then by Lemma C.1 of
Silva 2009
we have, since
X\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}N_{Y}
, that

\displaystyle I(Y;f(X))=I(f(X)+N_{Y};f(X))\geq I(f(X)^{\mathrm{G}}+N_{Y}^{\mathrm{G}};f(X)^{\mathrm{G}}),

Note, we have equality if and only if
f(X)
and
N_{Y}
are jointly Gaussian. Furthermore,

\displaystyle I(f(X)^{\mathrm{G}}+N_{Y}^{\mathrm{G}};f(X)^{\mathrm{G}})

\displaystyle=h(f(X)^{\mathrm{G}}+N_{Y}^{\mathrm{G}})-h(f(X)^{\mathrm{G}}+N_{Y}^{\mathrm{G}}|f(X)^{\mathrm{G}})

\displaystyle=h(f(X)^{\mathrm{G}}+N_{Y}^{\mathrm{G}})-h(N_{Y}^{\mathrm{G}})

\displaystyle=\log(\sqrt{2\pi(\mathrm{Var}(f(X))+\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y}))})-\log(\sqrt{2\pi\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y})})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{Var}(f(X))+\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y})}{\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y})}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(1+\frac{\mathrm{Var}(f(X))}{\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y})}\right).

(b)

If
f(X)+N_{Y}
is log-concave distributed, then by Theorem 3 of
Marsiglietti and Kostina 2018
we have that

\displaystyle h(f(X)+N_{Y})\geq\frac{1}{2}\log\left(4\mathrm{Var}(f(X)+N_{Y})\right)=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(4(\mathrm{Var}(f(X))+\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y})\right).

Furthermore, it is well known that for fixed variance, the normal distribution maximizes entropy, hence

\displaystyle h(N_{Y})\leq h(N_{Y}^{\mathrm{G}})=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(2\pi\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y})\right).

Therefore, we get that

\displaystyle I(Y;f(X))

\displaystyle=I(f(X)+N_{Y};f(X))

\displaystyle=h(f(X)+N_{Y})-h(f(X)+N_{Y}|f(X))

\displaystyle=h(f(X)+N_{Y})-h(N_{Y})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\log\left(4(\mathrm{Var}(f(X))+\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y})\right)-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(2\pi e\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{2}{\pi e}+\frac{2}{\pi e}\frac{\mathrm{Var}(f(X))}{\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y})}\right),

which yields a strictly positive lower bound if and only if

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\pi e}+\frac{2}{\pi e}\frac{\mathrm{Var}(f(X))}{\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y})}>1\iff\frac{\mathrm{Var}(f(X))}{\mathrm{Var}(N_{Y})}>\frac{\pi e}{2}-1\approx 3.27.

\square

Lemma C.6
.

Two different but Markov equivalent trees
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
and
\hat{\mathcal{G}}
share the exact same edges except for a single reversed directed path between the two root nodes of the graphs,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rcccccccccc}\hat{\mathcal{G}}:&c_{1}&\to&c_{2}&\to&\cdots&\to&c_{r-1}&\to&c_{r},\\
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}:&c_{r}&\to&c_{r-1}&\to&\cdots&\to&c_{2}&\to&c_{1},\end{array}

with
c_{1}=\mathrm{rt}(\hat{\mathcal{G}})
and
c_{r}=\mathrm{rt}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})
.

Proof of
Lemma

C.6
:
First, note that there always exists a unique directed path in
\hat{\mathcal{G}}
from
\mathrm{rt}(\hat{\mathcal{G}})
to
\mathrm{rt}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{G}}:\mathrm{rt}(\hat{\mathcal{G}})=c_{1}\to\cdots\to c_{r-1}\to c_{r}=\mathrm{rt}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}).

Since
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
and
\hat{\mathcal{G}}
are Markov equivalent, they share the same skeleton, so in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
the above path must be reversed. That is, there exists a unique directed path in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
from
\mathrm{rt}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})
to
\mathrm{rt}(\hat{\mathcal{G}})
given by

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}:\mathrm{rt}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})=c_{r}\to c_{r-1}\to\cdots\to c_{1}=\mathrm{rt}(\hat{\mathcal{G}}),

If
r=p
we are done, so assume
r<p
. As
\hat{\mathcal{G}}
is a directed tree there must exists a node
z_{2}
which is not a part of the above path but is a child of a node in the path. That is, there exists a node
z_{1}\in\{c_{1},\ldots,c_{r}\}
such that
\hat{\mathcal{G}}
contains the edge

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{G}}:z_{1}\to z_{2}.

Furthermore, by equality of skeleton, this edge must also be present in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}:z_{1}-z_{2}.

Assume for contradiction that
z_{2}\to z_{1}
in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
. As such, it must hold that
z_{1}=c_{r}=\mathrm{rt}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})
for otherwise if
z_{1}\in\{c_{1},\ldots,c_{r-1}\}
then
z_{1}
would have two parents in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
, a contradiction since
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
is a directed tree. However, if
z_{1}=c_{r}=\mathrm{rt}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})
then there is an incoming edge into the root node, a contradiction. We conclude that the directed edge
z_{1}\to z_{2}
also is present in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
.

Any paths further out on this branch will coincide in both graphs for otherwise there exists nodes with two parents. These arguments show that any paths branching out from the main reversed path will coincide in both
\hat{\mathcal{G}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
. Thus, the two graphs coincide up to a directed path between root nodes that is reversed.

\square

Proof of
Proposition

4.3
:

By
Lemma

C.6
there exists a path reversal

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rcccccccccc}\mathcal{G}:&\mathrm{rt}(\mathcal{G})=c_{1}&\to&c_{2}&\to&\cdots&\to&c_{r-1}&\to&c_{r}=\mathrm{rt}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}),\\
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}:&\mathrm{rt}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})=c_{r}&\to&c_{r-1}&\to&\cdots&\to&c_{2}&\to&c_{1}=\mathrm{rt}(\mathcal{G}),\end{array}

while all other edges in
\mathcal{G}=(V,\mathcal{E})
and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}=(V,\tilde{\mathcal{E}})
coincide. The entropy score gap is therefore only concerning the root nodes and the reversed edges in the above path. That is

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle=h(X_{\mathrm{rt}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})})+\sum_{(j,i)\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}}h(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}|X_{j}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad-h(X_{\mathrm{rt}(\mathcal{G})})-\sum_{(j,i)\in\mathcal{E}}h(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}|X_{j}])

\displaystyle=h(X_{c_{r}})+\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}h(X_{c_{i}}-\mathbb{E}[X_{c_{i}}|X_{c_{i+1}}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad-h(X_{c_{1}})-\sum_{i=2}^{r}h(X_{c_{i}}-\mathbb{E}[X_{c_{i}}|X_{c_{i-1}}]).

Note that

\displaystyle h(X_{c_{r}})-h(X_{c_{1}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=2}^{r}h(X_{c_{i}})-\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}h(X_{c_{i}})=\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}h(X_{c_{i+1}})-h(X_{c_{i}}).

Hence

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}h(X_{c_{i}}-\mathbb{E}[X_{c_{i}}|X_{c_{i+1}}])+h(X_{c_{i+1}})-h(X_{c_{i+1}}-\mathbb{E}[X_{c_{i+1}}|X_{c_{i}}])-h(X_{c_{i}})

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}\Delta\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(c_{i}\mathrel{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hss\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{$\longrightarrow$}\hss\cr\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\dashleftarrow$}\crcr}}}}c_{i+1})

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle\min_{1\leq i\leq r-1}\Delta\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(c_{i}\mathrel{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hss\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{$\longrightarrow$}\hss\cr\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\dashleftarrow$}\crcr}}}}c_{i+1}),

which concludes the proof.

\square

C.50.4.2
Proof of
Theorem

4.5

We first describe the graphs that result from the reduction
technique described in
4.22.3
.
To do so, define

\mathbb{L}(\mathcal{G},\tilde{\mathcal{G}}):=\{L\in V_{R}:{\mathrm{ch}^{\mathcal{G}_{R}}({L})}=\emptyset\land({\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}}({L})}\not={\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}_{R}}({L})}\lor{\mathrm{ch}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}}({L})}\not=\emptyset)\},

containing
the sink nodes in
\mathcal{G}_{R}

that are either not sink nodes in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
or sink nodes in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
with different parents:
{\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}_{R}}({L})}\not={\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}}({L})}
.
Now fix any
L\in\mathbb{L}(\mathcal{G},\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\subseteq V_{R}
and note that its only parent in
\mathcal{G}_{R}
,
{\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}_{R}}({L})}
, is either also a parent of
L
, a child of
L
or not adjacent to
L
, in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
. That is, one and only one of the following sets
is non-empty

\displaystyle Z(L):

\displaystyle={\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}_{R}}({L})}\cap{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}}({L})},

(‘staying parents’)

\displaystyle Y(L):

\displaystyle={\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}_{R}}({L})}\cap{\mathrm{ch}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}}({L})},

(‘parents to children’)

\displaystyle W(L):

\displaystyle={\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}_{R}}({L})}\cap(V\setminus\{L\cup{\mathrm{ch}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}}({L})}\cup{\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}}({L})}\})

(‘removing parents’)

We define the
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
parent and children of
L
that are not adjacent to
L
in
\mathcal{G}_{R}
as

\displaystyle D(L):

\displaystyle={\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}}({L})}\cap(V\setminus\{L\cup{\mathrm{ch}^{\mathcal{G}_{R}}({L})}\cup{\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}_{R}}({L})}\}),\text{ and}

\displaystyle O(L):

\displaystyle={\mathrm{ch}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}}({L})}\cap(V\setminus\{L\cup{\mathrm{ch}^{\mathcal{G}_{R}}({L})}\cup{\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}_{R}}({L})}\}),

respectively.
All such sets contain at most one node and
by slight abuse of notation,
we use the same letters to refer to the nodes.
We will henceforth suppress the dependence on
L
if the choice is clear from the context.

Figure

4.1
visualizes the above sets.

Now partition
\mathcal{T}_{p}\setminus\{\mathcal{G}\}
into the three following disjoint partitions for which there exists a reduced graph sink node
L\in\mathbb{L}(\mathcal{G},\tilde{\mathcal{G}})
such that
W(L)
,
Y(L)
and
Z(L)
is non-empty, respectively. That is, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W):

\displaystyle=\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}\setminus\{\mathcal{G}\}:\exists L\in\mathbb{L}(\mathcal{G},\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\text{ s.t. }W(L)\not=\emptyset\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Y):

\displaystyle=\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}\setminus\{\mathcal{G}\}:\exists L\in\mathbb{L}(\mathcal{G},\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\text{ s.t. }Y(L)\not=\emptyset\}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W),

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Z):

\displaystyle=\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}\setminus\{\mathcal{G}\}:\exists L\in\mathbb{L}(\mathcal{G},\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\text{ s.t. }Z(L)\not=\emptyset\}\setminus(\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W)\cup\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Y)).

Using that
\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W)\cup\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Y)\cup\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Z)=\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G})
, we can now find a lower bound for the score gap that holds uniformly over all alternative directed tree graphs
\mathcal{T}_{p}\setminus\{\mathcal{G}\}
:

\displaystyle\min_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}\setminus\{\mathcal{G}\}}\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})=

\displaystyle\,\min\bigg\{\min_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Z)}\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G}),

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\min_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W)}\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G}),\min_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Y)}\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})\bigg\}.

We now turn to each of these three terms individually and first consider alternative graphs in the partitioning
\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Z)
. The following lower bound consists of possibly non-localized conditional dependence properties of the observable distribution
P_{X}
. (That is, the bound may involve nodes that are not close to each other in the graph
\mathcal{G}
.)

Lemma C.7
.

Let
\Pi_{Z}(\mathcal{G})
denote all tuples
(z,l,o)\in V^{3}
of adjacent nodes
(z\to l)\in\mathcal{E}
for which there exists a node
o\in{\mathrm{nd}^{\mathcal{G}}({l})}\setminus\{z,l\}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\min_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Z)}\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\geq\min_{(z,l,o)\in\Pi_{Z}(\mathcal{G})}I(X_{z};X_{o}|X_{l}).

The next result proves a lower bound that holds uniformly over all alternative graphs in
\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W)
. The lower bound consists only of local conditional dependence properties. That is, for any subgraph of the causal graph
\mathcal{G}
of the form
X_{o}\to X_{w}\to X_{l}
or
X_{o}\leftarrow X_{w}\to X_{l}
we measure, by means of conditional mutual information, the conditional dependence of the two adjacent nodes
X_{w}
and
X_{l}
conditional on
X_{o}
,
I(X_{w};X_{l}|X_{o})
. The lower bound consists of the smallest of all such local conditional dependence measures.

Lemma C.8
.

Let
\Pi_{W}(\mathcal{G})
denote all tuples
(w,l,o)\in V^{3}
of adjacent nodes
(w\to l)\in\mathcal{E}
and
o\in({\mathrm{ch}^{\mathcal{G}}({w})}\setminus\{l\})\cup{\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}}({w})}
. It holds that that

\displaystyle\min_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W)}\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\geq\min_{(w,l,o)\in\Pi_{W}(\mathcal{G})}I(X_{w};X_{l}|X_{o}).

A uniform lower bound of the score gap over all alternative graphs in the final partition
\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Y)
is given by the smallest edge-reversal of any edge in the causal graph
\mathcal{G}
.

Lemma C.9
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\min_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Y)}\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})\geq\min_{(j\to i)\in\mathcal{E}}\Delta\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(j\mathrel{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hss\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{$\longrightarrow$}\hss\cr\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\dashleftarrow$}\crcr}}}}i).

An immediate consequence of
Lemmas

C.7
,
C.8
and
C.9
is that the entropy identifiability gap is given by the smallest of the lower bounds derived for each partition, see
Theorem

4.5
. Thus, it only remains to prove
Lemmas

C.7
,
C.8
and
C.9
.

Proof of
Lemma

C.7
:

Let
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\Pi_{Z}(\mathcal{G})
such that
Z\not=\emptyset
. This implies that
Y=W=\emptyset
as
L
can only have one parent in
\mathcal{G}
. Furthermore,
D=\emptyset
as
L
can only have one parent in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
and
O\not=\emptyset
for otherwise
L
would have been deleted by the deletion procedure in
Section

4.22
. Assume without loss of generality that
O=\{O_{1},\ldots,O_{k}\}
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
. The two subgraphs are illustrated in
Figure

C.6
.

V_{1}=\{Z,L\}^{c}
Z
L
\mathcal{G}_{R}

\tilde{V}_{1}=\{Z,L,O,\mathcal{A}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{A}_{k}\}^{c}
Z
L
\vdots
O_{1}
O_{k}
\mathcal{A}_{1}
\mathcal{A}_{k}
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}

Figure C.6:
Illustration of the reduced form graphs
\mathcal{G}_{R}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
for the case
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\Pi_{Z}(\mathcal{G})
.
\mathcal{A}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{A}_{k}
are possibly empty sets of nodes, and dashed rectangle nodes denotes a possibly multi-node subgraph over the variables enclosed. The bi-directed edges means that the edge can be directed in both directions. An edge pointing into the multi-node subgraph, can possibly be multiple edges into distinct nodes of the subgraph.

For ease of notation, fix any
1\leq i\leq k
and denote
O:=O_{i}
. We note that in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
the following d-separation holds

\displaystyle Z\,{\perp\!\!\!\!\perp_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}}}O\,|\,L.

Thus, we have for all probability measures
Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}
over nodes
V
that
Z\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}O\,|\,L
(as the path between
Z
and
O
is blocked by
L
and all probability measures generated in accordance with an SCM are Markovian with respect to the generating graph
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
). Recall that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle=\inf_{Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{X}\|Q)

\displaystyle=\inf_{Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}}h(P_{X},Q)-h(P_{X}).

Fix
Q=q\cdot\lambda^{p}\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}
and note that it factorizes as
Q=Q_{A|Z,O,L}Q_{Z|L}Q_{O|L}Q_{L}
, i.e., the density
q
factorizes as

\displaystyle q(x)

\displaystyle=q_{A|Z,O,L}(a|z,o,l)q_{Z,O,L}(z,o,l)

\displaystyle=q_{A|Z,O,L}(a|z,o,l)q_{Z|L}(z|l)q_{O|L}(o|l)q_{L}(l),

for
\lambda^{p}
-almost all
x=(a,z,o,l)\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
where
A=V\setminus\{Z,O,L\}
. Hence, the cross entropy splits additively into

\displaystyle h(P_{X},Q)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}[-\log(q_{A|Z,O,L}(A|Z,O,L))]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{E}[-\log(q_{Z|L}(Z|L))]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{E}[-\log(q_{O|L}(O|L))]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{E}[-\log(q_{L}(L))].

(C.26)

Now note, e.g., that for a conditional distribution (Markov kernel)
Q_{Z|L}
it holds that

\displaystyle 0\leq D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{Z|L}P_{L}\|Q_{Z|L}P_{L})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[-\log\left(\frac{q_{Z|L}(Z|L)p_{L}(L)}{p_{Z|L}(Z|L)p_{L}(L)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[-\log(q_{Z|L}(Z|L))]-\mathbb{E}[-\log(p_{Z|L}(Z|L))],

proving that

\mathbb{E}[-\log(q_{Z|L}(Z|L))]\geq\mathbb{E}[-\log(p_{Z|L}(Z|L))].

By similar arguments, we get that the three other terms in the lower bound of
Equation

C.26
are bounded below by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[-\log(q_{A|Z,O,L}(A|Z,O,L))]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}[-\log(p_{A|Z,O,L}(A|Z,O,L))],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[-\log(q_{O|L}(O|L))]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}[-\log(p_{O|L}(O|L))],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[-\log(q_{L}(L))]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}[-\log(p_{L}(L))].

This implies that

\displaystyle\inf_{Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}}h(P_{X},Q)\geq h(P_{X},Q^{*}),

where
Q^{*}=P_{A|Z,O,L}P_{Z|L}P_{O|L}P_{L}
. On the other hand, we know that
P_{X}
factorizes as
P_{X}=P_{A|Z,O,L}P_{Z,O|L}P_{L}
. Thus we have the following entropy score gap lower bound

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\geq h(P_{X},Q^{*})-h(P_{X})

\displaystyle=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{X}\|Q^{*})

\displaystyle=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{A|Z,O,L}P_{Z,O|L}P_{L}\|P_{A|Z,O,L}P_{Z|L}P_{O|L}P_{L})

\displaystyle=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{Z,O|L}P_{L}\|P_{Z|L}P_{O|L}P_{L})

\displaystyle=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{Z,O|L}\|P_{Z|L}P_{O|L}|P_{L})

\displaystyle=I(Z;O|L).

\Pi_{Z}(\mathcal{G})
denotes all tuples
(z,l,o)\in V^{3}
of adjacent nodes
(z\to l)\in\mathcal{E}
for which there exists a node
o\in{\mathrm{nd}^{\mathcal{G}}({l})}\setminus\{z,l\}
. For any graph
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Z)
we can, by the above considerations, find a tuple
(z,l,o)\in\Pi_{Z}(\mathcal{G})
such that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})\geq I(X_{o};X_{z}\,|\,X_{l}).

We conclude that

\displaystyle\min_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Z)}\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\geq\min_{(z,l,o)\in\Pi_{Z}(\mathcal{G})}I(X_{o};X_{z}|X_{l}).

\square

Proof of
Lemma

C.8
:

Fix any
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W)
and
L
with
W\not=\emptyset
such that
Z=Y=\emptyset
. We have illustrated the subgraph
\mathcal{G}_{R}
in
Figure

C.7
and the possible subgraphs
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
in
Figure

C.8
.

\{W,L\}^{c}
W
L
\mathcal{G}_{R}

Figure C.7:
Illustrations of the
\mathcal{G}_{R}
subgraph for for
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W)
.

L
\vdots
O_{1}
O_{k}
\mathcal{A}_{1}
\mathcal{A}_{k}
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
:
D=\emptyset
,
O\not=\emptyset

\{L,D\}^{c}
D
L
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
:
D\not=\emptyset
,
O=\emptyset

\{D,L,O,\mathcal{A}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{A}_{k}\}^{c}
D
L
\vdots
O_{1}
O_{k}
\mathcal{A}_{1}
\mathcal{A}_{k}
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
:
D\not=\emptyset
,
O\not=\emptyset

Figure C.8:
Illustrations of the possible
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
subgraphs for
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W)
.

Note that for any of the three possible local graph structures presented in
Figure

C.8
there exists an
A\in\{O_{1},\ldots,O_{k},D\}
such that
L\,{\perp\!\!\!\!\perp_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}}}W\,|\,A
, i.e., A blocks the path between
L
and
W
. Thus, for all probability measures
Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}
over nodes
V=\{1,..,p\}
it always holds that
L\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}W\,|\,A
. By arguments similar to those in the proof of
Lemma

C.7
, we note that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle=\inf_{Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}}h(P_{X},Q)-h(P_{X}),

and that

\inf_{Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}}h(P_{X},Q)\geq h(P_{X},Q^{*}),

for
P_{X}=P_{K|W,L,A}P_{W,L|A}P_{A}
and
Q^{*}=P_{K|W,L,A}P_{L|A}P_{W|A}P_{A}
where
K=V\setminus\{W,L,A\}
. To that end, we now have that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\geq h(P_{X},Q^{*})-h(P_{X})

\displaystyle=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{X}\|Q^{*})

\displaystyle=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{K|W,L,A}P_{W,L|A}P_{A}\|P_{K|W,L,A}P_{L|A}P_{W|A}P_{A})

\displaystyle=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{W,L|A}P_{A}\|P_{L|A}P_{W|A}P_{A})

\displaystyle=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{W,L|A}\|P_{L|A}P_{W|A}|P_{A})

\displaystyle=I(W;L|A).

Let
\hat{\Pi}_{W}(\mathcal{G})
denote all tuples
(w,l,a)\in V^{3}
of adjacent nodes
(w\to l)\in\mathcal{E}
for which there exists a node
a\in{\mathrm{nd}^{\mathcal{G}}({l})}\setminus\{w\}
. Now note that for any graph
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W)
we can, by the above considerations, find a tuple
(w,l,a)\in\hat{\Pi}_{W}(\mathcal{G})
such that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})\geq I(X_{w};X_{l}\,|\,X_{a}).

(C.27)

(Conversely for any tuple
(w,l,a)\in\hat{\Pi}_{W}(\mathcal{G})
we can construct a graph
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W)
such that (
C.27
) holds. To see this, fix
(w,l,a)\in\hat{\Pi}_{W}(\mathcal{G})
and construct
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
such that the subtree with root node
l
is identical in both
\mathcal{G}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
and
a
blocks the path between
l
and
w
in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
.) Therefore, the following lower bound holds (and it is not unnecessarily small).

\displaystyle\min_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W)}\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})\geq\min_{(w,l,a)\in\hat{\Pi}_{W}(\mathcal{G})}I(X_{w};X_{l}\,|\,X_{a}).

For any
(w,l,a)\in\hat{\Pi}_{W}(\mathcal{G})
it either holds that
a\in({\mathrm{ch}^{\mathcal{G}}({w})}\setminus\{l\})\cup{\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}}({w})}
or that there exists an
o\in({\mathrm{ch}^{\mathcal{G}}({w})}\setminus\{l\})\cup{\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}}({w})}
blocking the path between
a
and
l
in
\mathcal{G}
such that
X_{l}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}X_{a}|X_{o}
. Furthermore, we note that as
X_{l}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}(X_{o},X_{a})\,|\,X_{w}
we have that

\displaystyle I(X_{w};X_{l}|X_{a})

\displaystyle=h(X_{l}|X_{a})-h(X_{l}|X_{a},X_{w})

\displaystyle=h(X_{l}|X_{a})-h(X_{l}|X_{w})

\displaystyle=h(X_{l}|X_{a})-h(X_{l}|X_{o},X_{w})

\displaystyle\geq h(X_{l}|X_{a},X_{o})-h(X_{l}|X_{o},X_{w})

\displaystyle=h(X_{l}|X_{o})-h(X_{l}|X_{o},X_{w})

\displaystyle=I(X_{w};X_{l}\,|\,X_{o}),

as further conditioning reduces conditional entropy. Let
\Pi_{W}(\mathcal{G})
denote all tuples
(w,l,o)\in V^{3}
of adjacent nodes
(w\to l)\in\mathcal{E}
and
o\in({\mathrm{ch}^{\mathcal{G}}({w})}\setminus\{l\})\cup{\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}}({w})}
. By the above considerations we conclude that

\displaystyle\min_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},W)}\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})\geq\min_{(w,l,o)\in\Pi_{W}(\mathcal{G})}I(X_{w};X_{l}\,|\,X_{o}).

\square

Proof of
Lemma

C.9
:

Fix
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Y)
and
L
such that
Y\not=\emptyset
. It holds that
W=Z=\emptyset
. We have illustrated the
\mathcal{G}_{R}
in
Figure

C.9
and the three possible subgraphs
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
in
Figure

C.10
.

\{Y,L\}^{c}
Y
L
\mathcal{G}_{R}

Figure C.9:
Illustrations of the
\mathcal{G}_{R}
subgraph for
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Y)
.

\{Y,L,O\}^{c}
L
Y
O
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
:
D=\emptyset
,
O\not=\emptyset

\{Y,L,D\}^{c}
D
L
Y
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
:
D\not=\emptyset
,
O=\emptyset

\{D,L,Y,O,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\}^{c}
D
L
Y
O
\mathcal{A}
\mathcal{B}
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
:
D\not=\emptyset
,
O\not=\emptyset

Figure C.10:
Illustrations of the possible
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
subgraphs for
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Y)
.

Note that for any of the three possible local graph structures of
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
illustrated in
Figure

C.10
we have that for all probability measures
Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}
factorizes as
Q_{A|L,Y}Q_{L,Y}
, where
A=V\setminus\{L,Y\}
. It always holds that
Q_{L,Y}
is the distribution of
(\tilde{L},\tilde{Y})
generated in accordance with a structural causal model of the form

\displaystyle\tilde{Y}:=\tilde{f}_{Y}(\tilde{L})+\tilde{N}_{Y},

(C.28)

where
\tilde{f}_{Y}(l)=\mathbb{E}[Y|L=l]
for all
l\in\mathbb{R}
, and any
\mathcal{L}(\tilde{N}_{Y}),\mathcal{L}(\tilde{L})\in\mathcal{P}
with
\tilde{N}_{Y}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\tilde{L}
. Now recall that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle=\inf_{Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}}h(P_{X},Q)-h(P_{X}),

and notice that by arguments similar to those in the proof of
Lemma

C.7
we get

\displaystyle h(P_{X},Q)

\displaystyle=h(P_{X},Q_{A|L,Y}Q_{L,Y})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[-\log(q_{A|L,Y}(A|L,Y))]+h(P_{L,Y},Q_{L,Y})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}[-\log(p_{A|L,Y}(A|L,Y))]+h(P_{L,Y},Q_{L,Y}),

and that
h(P_{X})=\mathbb{E}[-\log(p_{A|L,Y}(A|L,Y))]+h(P_{L,Y})
. Thus, we have that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})\geq\inf_{Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}}h(P_{L,Y},Q_{L,Y})-h(P_{L,Y}).

For any
Q=Q_{A|L,Y}Q_{L,Y}\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}
we have that
Q_{L,Y}
is uniquely determined by a marginal distribution
Q_{L}\in\mathcal{P}
and the noise distribution of
\tilde{N}_{Y}\sim q_{\tilde{N}_{Y}}\cdot\lambda\in\mathcal{P}
from the additive noise structural assignment in
Equation

C.28
for
\tilde{Y}
and the causal function
\tilde{f}_{Y}
. Thus, the density
q_{L,Y}
of
Q_{L,Y}
is given by

\displaystyle q_{L,Y}(l,y)=q_{Y|L}(y|l)q_{L}(l)=q_{\tilde{N}_{Y}}(y-\tilde{f}_{Y}(l))q_{L}(l)=q_{\tilde{N}_{Y}}(y-\mathbb{E}[Y|L=l])q_{L}(l).

Hence,

\displaystyle h(P_{L,Y},Q_{L,Y})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[-\log\left(q_{L,Y}(L,Y)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[-\log\left(q_{Y|L}(Y|L)\right)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[-\log\left(q_{L}(L)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[-\log\left(q_{\tilde{N}_{Y}}(Y-\mathbb{E}[Y|L])\right)\right]+h(P_{L},Q_{L})

\displaystyle=h(Y-\mathbb{E}[Y|L],\tilde{N}_{Y})+h(P_{L},Q_{L})

\displaystyle\geq h(Y-\mathbb{E}[Y|L])+h(L),

where we used that
h(P,Q)=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P,Q)+h(P)\geq h(P)
. Thus, we have that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}}h(P_{L,Y},Q_{L,Y})-h(P_{L,Y})

\displaystyle\geq h(Y-\mathbb{E}[Y|L])+h(L)-h(L-\mathbb{E}[L|Y])-h(Y)

\displaystyle=\Delta\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(Y\mathrel{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hss\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{$\longrightarrow$}\hss\cr\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\dashleftarrow$}\crcr}}}}L).

We conclude that

\displaystyle\min_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Y)}\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G})\geq\min_{(i\to j)\in\mathcal{E}}\Delta\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(j\mathrel{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hss\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{$\longrightarrow$}\hss\cr\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\dashleftarrow$}\crcr}}}}i).

\square

C.50.4.3
Remaining proof of Section
4.22

Proof of
Theorem

4.6
:
Consider a graph
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{G},Z)
and let
\mathcal{G}_{R,1}=(\mathcal{E}_{R,1},V_{R,1})
and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1}=(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{R,1},V_{R,1})
be the reduced graphs after the initial edge and node deletion procedure of
Section

4.22.3
. The deletion procedure does not change the score gap, that is,

\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G})=\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G}_{R,1}).

For any
i\geq 1
and fixed
\mathcal{G}_{R,i}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,i}
we define

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{R,i}:=\{L\in V_{R,i}:{\mathrm{ch}^{\mathcal{G}_{R,i}}({L})}=\emptyset\land({\mathrm{pa}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,i}}({L})}\not={\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}_{R,i}}({L})}\lor{\mathrm{ch}^{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,i}}({L})}\not=\emptyset)\}.

Now fix
L_{1}\in\mathbb{L}_{R,1}
such that
Z_{1}\not=\emptyset
, where
Y_{1},Z_{1},W_{1},D_{1}
and
O_{1}
are defined similarly to the variables in
Section

4.22
. Let
O_{1}=\{O_{1,1},\ldots,O_{1,k_{1}}\}
, for some
k_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
.

Assume that there exists an
i\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}
such that
(Z_{1}\to O_{1,i})\in\mathcal{E}_{R,1}
in which case we have the following two paths in
\mathcal{G}_{R,1}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1}

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{R,1}:O_{1,i}\leftarrow Z_{1}\to L_{1},\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1}:Z_{1}\to L_{1}\to O_{1,i}.

Since
O_{1,i}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1}}Z_{1}\,|\,L_{1}
, an entropy score gap lower bound is given by

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G}_{R,1})

\displaystyle\geq\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G}_{R,1})

\displaystyle=\inf_{Q\in\{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\}\times\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})\times\mathcal{P}^{p}}h(P_{X},Q)-P_{X}

\displaystyle\geq D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{X}\|Q^{*})

\displaystyle=I(O_{1,i};Z_{1}|L_{1}),

with
P_{X}=P_{K|O,Z,L}P_{O,Z|L}P_{L}
and
Q^{*}=P_{K|O,Z,L}P_{Z|L}P_{O|L}P_{L}
for
K=V\setminus\{O,Z,L\}
, by arguments similar to those from the proof of
Lemma

C.8
. Now note that
(Z_{1},O_{1,i},L_{1})\in\Pi_{W}(\mathcal{G}_{R,1})\subseteq\Pi_{W}(\mathcal{G})
as
(Z_{1}\to O_{1,i})\in\mathcal{E}_{R,1}
and
L_{1}\in{\mathrm{ch}^{\mathcal{G}_{R,1}}({Z_{1}})}\setminus\{O_{1,i}\}\subseteq({\mathrm{ch}^{\mathcal{G}_{R,1}}({Z_{1}})}\setminus\{O_{1,i}\})\cup{\mathrm{pa}^{\mathcal{G}_{R,1}}({Z_{1}})}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G}_{R,1})\geq\min_{(w,l,o)\in\Pi_{W}(\mathcal{G})}I(X_{w};X_{l}|X_{o}).

(C.29)

Assume now that for all
i\in\{1,..,k_{1}\}
we have
(Z_{1}\to O_{1,i})\not\in\mathcal{E}_{R,1}
. Let
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1}=(\hat{\mathcal{E}}_{R,1},V_{R,1})
denote an intermediate graph where
\hat{\mathcal{E}}_{R,1}
is identical to
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{R,1}
except the edges
\{(L_{1}\to O_{1,i}):1\leq i\leq k_{1}\}\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{R,1}
are replaced by the edges
\{(Z_{1}\to O_{1,i}):1\leq i\leq k_{1}\}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G}_{R,1})

\displaystyle=\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1})+\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G}_{R,1})

\displaystyle\geq\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G}_{R,1}).

Note that this score gap lower bound is still strictly positive as
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1}\not=\mathcal{G}_{R,1}
.
To realize the last inequality (see also
Peters et al. 2022
), simply note that as
O_{1,i}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}L_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}
we have for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}
that

\displaystyle 2\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1},O_{1,i})

\displaystyle=\log\mathbb{E}[(O_{1,i}-\mathbb{E}[O_{1,i}|L_{1}])^{2}]

\displaystyle\geq\log\mathbb{E}[(O_{1,i}-\mathbb{E}[O_{1,i}|Z_{1},L_{1}])^{2}]

\displaystyle=\log\mathbb{E}[(O_{1,i}-\mathbb{E}[O_{1,i}|Z_{1}])^{2}]

\displaystyle=2\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1},O_{1,i}).

(C.30)

Now since all edges in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1}
and
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1}
coincide except the incoming edges into
O_{1,1},\ldots,O_{1,k_{1}}
we get that

\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1})=\sum_{i=1}^{k_{1}}\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1},O_{1,i})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1},O_{1,i})\geq 0,

where the inequality follows from
Equation

C.30
.
Now both
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1}
and
\mathcal{G}_{R,1}
have a childless node
L_{1}
with the same parent
Z_{1}
, so we let
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,2}
and
\mathcal{G}_{R,2}
denote these two graphs where the node
L_{1}
and its incoming edge are deleted. This deletion does not change the graph scores, i.e.,

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{R,1})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G}_{R,1})

\displaystyle=\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,2})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G}_{R,2}).

Now fix
L_{2}\in\mathbb{L}_{R,2}
and define
Y_{2},Z_{2},W_{2},D_{2}
and
O_{2}=\{O_{2,1},\ldots,O_{2,k_{2}}\}
accordingly.

If either
Y_{2}
or
W_{2}
is non-empty, we use the score gap lower bound previously discussed in
Lemma

C.8
and
Lemma

C.9
. If
Z_{2}
is non-empty, we can repeat the above procedure and iteratively move edges and delete nodes until we arrive at the first
i\in\mathbb{N}
with
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,i}
and
\mathcal{G}_{R,i}
being the iteratively reduced graphs and
L_{R,i}\in\mathbb{L}_{R,i}
where either

i)

Y_{i}
or
W_{i}
is non-empty, here, we get that
\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G})
is lower bounded by a bound similar to the form of
Lemma

C.8
or
Lemma

C.9
. That is,

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,i})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G}_{R,i})\geq

\displaystyle\,\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,i})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G}_{R,i})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\,\min\left\{\min_{j\to i\in\mathcal{E}}\Delta\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(i\mathrel{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hss\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{$\longrightarrow$}\hss\cr\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\dashleftarrow$}\crcr}}}}j),\min_{(w,l,o)\in\Pi_{W}(\mathcal{G})}I(X_{w};X_{l}\,|\,X_{o})\right\}.

ii)

Z_{i}
is non-empty and there exists a
j\in\{1,\ldots,k_{i}\}
such that
(Z_{i}\to O_{i,j})\in\mathcal{G}_{R,i}
. As previously argued, the score gap lower bound of
Equation

C.29
applies. That is

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,i})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G}_{R,i})\geq\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{R,i})-\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathcal{G}_{R,i})

\displaystyle\geq\min_{(w,l,o)\in\Pi_{W}(\mathcal{G})}I(X_{w};X_{l}\,|\,X_{o}).

Note that whenever we do not meet scenario i) or ii) we remove a node in both graphs that is a sink node in the reduced true causal graph
\mathcal{G}_{R,i}
and the intermediate graph
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{R,i}
. After at most
p-2
graph reduction iterations of not encountering scenario i) or ii) we are left with two different graphs on two nodes, in which case the score gap is an edge reversal.
We conclude that

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{G}})-\ell_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\geq\min\left\{\min_{i\to j\in\mathcal{E}}\Delta\ell_{\mathrm{E}}(j\mathrel{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hss\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{$\longrightarrow$}\hss\cr\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\dashleftarrow$}\crcr}}}}i),\min_{(w,l,o)\in\Pi_{W}(\mathcal{G})}I(X_{w};X_{l}\,|\,X_{o})\right\}.

\square
